1	use clap::{ArgAction, Parser, ValueEnum};
2	use opencascade_sys::{top_abs, top_exp};

5	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
6	// Stage
7	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
8	
9	/// Stage identifier as (major, minor). Minor=0 means run all sub-stages of that major stage.
10	/// Examples: (1, 2) = stage 1.2, (2, 0) = all of stage 2, (4, 1) = full pipeline.
11	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
12	pub struct Stage(pub u8, pub u8);
13	
14	impl Stage {
15	    /// The final sub-stage for each major stage.
16	    fn last_minor(major: u8) -> u8 {
17	        match major {
18	            1 => 3,
19	            2 => 7,
20	            3 => 6,
21	            4 => 1,
22	            _ => 0,
23	        }
24	    }
25	
26	    /// Normalize: if minor is 0, expand to last sub-stage of that major.
27	    pub fn normalized(self) -> (u8, u8) {
28	        if self.1 == 0 {
29	            (self.0, Self::last_minor(self.0))
30	        } else {
31	            (self.0, self.1)
32	        }
33	    }
34	
35	    /// Returns true if the pipeline should run at least through the given stage.
36	    pub fn at_least(&self, major: u8, minor: u8) -> bool {
37	        let (sm, ss) = self.normalized();
38	        (sm, ss) >= (major, minor)
39	    }
40	}
41	
42	impl Default for Stage {
43	    fn default() -> Self {
44	        Stage(4, 1)
45	    }
46	}
47	
48	fn parse_stage(s: &str) -> Result<Stage, String> {
49	    if let Some((major_s, minor_s)) = s.split_once('.') {
50	        let major: u8 = major_s.parse().map_err(|_| format!("invalid stage: {s}"))?;
51	        let minor: u8 = minor_s.parse().map_err(|_| format!("invalid stage: {s}"))?;
52	        if !(1..=4).contains(&major) || !(1..=Stage::last_minor(major)).contains(&minor) {
53	            return Err(format!("invalid stage: {s} (valid: 1.1–4.1)"));
54	        }
55	        Ok(Stage(major, minor))
56	    } else {
57	        let major: u8 = s.parse().map_err(|_| format!("invalid stage: {s}"))?;
58	        if !(1..=4).contains(&major) {
59	            return Err(format!("invalid stage: {s} (valid: 1–4)"));
60	        }
61	        Ok(Stage(major, 0))
62	    }
63	}

227	    pub verbosity: u8,

244	impl std::fmt::Debug for Config {
245	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
246	        f.debug_struct("Config")
247	            .field("input_stl", &self.input_stl)
248	            .field("output_step", &self.output_step)
249	            .field("stl_units", &self.stl_units)
250	            .field("step_units", &self.step_units)
251	            .field("compare_step", &self.compare_step)
252	            .field("compare_shape", &if self.compare_shape.is_some() { format!("[{} faces]", self.compare_face_count) } else { "None".to_string() })
253	            .field("vertex_tolerance_mm", &self.vertex_tolerance_mm)
254	            .field("surface_tolerance_mm", &self.surface_tolerance_mm)
255	            .field("angular_tolerance_rad", &self.angular_tolerance_rad)
256	            .field("verbosity", &self.verbosity)
257	            .field("verbose", &self.verbose)
258	            .field("quiet", &self.quiet)
259	            .field("strict_mesh", &self.strict_mesh)
260	            .field("debug", &self.debug)
261	            .field("stage", &self.stage)
262	            .field("viz_stages", &self.viz_stages)
263	            .field("viz_stages_level", &self.viz_stages_level)
264	            .finish()
265	    }
266	}
267	
268	impl Config {
269	    fn from_cli(args: CliArgs) -> Self {
270	        let scale = args.stl_units.to_mm();
271	        Self {
272	            input_stl: args.input_stl,
273	            output_step: args.output_step,
274	            stl_units: args.stl_units,
275	            step_units: args.step_units,
276	            compare_step: args.compare_step,
277	            compare_shape: None,
278	            compare_face_count: 0,
279	            vertex_tolerance_mm: args.vertex_tolerance * scale,
280	            surface_tolerance_mm: args.surface_tolerance * scale,
281	            angular_tolerance_rad: args.angular_tolerance.to_radians(),
282	            verbosity: args.verbose_level.unwrap_or(0).max(args.verbose_short),
283	            verbose: args.verbose_level.unwrap_or(0).max(args.verbose_short) >= 1,
284	            quiet: args.quiet,
285	            strict_mesh: args.strict_mesh,
286	            debug: args.debug,
287	            stage: args.stage.unwrap_or_default(),
288	            viz_stages: args.viz_stages.unwrap_or_default(),
289	            viz_stages_level: args.viz_stages_level,
290	        }
291	    }
292	
293	    /// Check if a specific stage should be visualized.
294	    pub fn viz_active(&self, major: u8, minor: u8) -> bool {
295	        self.viz_stages.iter().any(|s| {
296	            let (sm, ss) = s.normalized();
297	            sm == major && ss == minor
298	        })
299	    }
300	
301	    /// If --compare was specified, load the STEP file shape for bounded distance comparison.
302	    pub fn load_compare_step(&mut self) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
303	        let step_path = match &self.compare_step {
304	            Some(p) => p.clone(),
305	            None => return Ok(()),
306	        };
307	
308	        let step_shape = crate::read_step_file(&step_path)
309	            .map_err(|e| -> Box<dyn std::error::Error> { e.into() })?;
310	
311	        // Count faces for validation and status output
312	        let mut face_count = 0_usize;
313	        let mut explorer = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
314	            &step_shape,
315	            top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face,
316	            top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
317	        );
318	        while explorer.more() {
319	            face_count += 1;
320	            explorer.next();
321	        }
322	
323	        if face_count == 0 {
324	            return Err(format!("STEP file {step_path} contains no faces").into());
325	        }
326	
327	        if !self.quiet {
328	            eprintln!("Loaded {} faces from comparison STEP file", face_count);
329	        }
330	
331	        self.compare_face_count = face_count;
332	        self.compare_shape = Some(step_shape);
333	
334	        Ok(())
335	    }
336	}
337	/// Parse command-line arguments and return a resolved Config.
338	pub fn parse_config() -> Config {
339	    Config::from_cli(CliArgs::parse())
340	}
341	
342	/// Parse from an explicit argument iterator (for testing or embedding).
343	pub fn parse_config_from<I, T>(args: I) -> Result<Config, clap::Error>
344	where
345	    I: IntoIterator<Item = T>,
346	    T: Into<std::ffi::OsString> + Clone,
347	{
348	    CliArgs::try_parse_from(args).map(Config::from_cli)
349	}

352	// Tests
353	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

355	#[cfg(test)]
356	mod tests {
357	    use super::*;
358	
359	    fn parse(args: &[&str]) -> Config {
360	        let mut full = vec!["brepper"];
361	        full.extend_from_slice(args);
362	        parse_config_from(full).expect("parse should succeed")
363	    }
364	
365	    #[test]
366	    fn minimal_args() {
367	        let config = parse(&["input.stl"]);
368	        assert_eq!(config.input_stl, "input.stl");
369	        assert_eq!(config.stage, Stage(4, 1));
370	        assert!(!config.verbose);
371	    }
372	
373	    #[test]
374	    fn full_args() {
375	        let config = parse(&[
376	            "input.stl", "-o", "output.step", "--compare", "ref.step",
377	            "--stage=2.3", "-v", "--vertex-tolerance=1e-6",
378	        ]);
379	        assert_eq!(config.input_stl, "input.stl");
380	        assert_eq!(config.output_step.as_deref(), Some("output.step"));
381	        assert_eq!(config.compare_step.as_deref(), Some("ref.step"));
382	        assert_eq!(config.stage, Stage(2, 3));
383	        assert!(config.verbose);
384	        // vertex_tolerance is in STL units (default mm), so 1e-6 mm
385	        assert!((config.vertex_tolerance_mm - 1e-6).abs() < 1e-15);
386	    }
387	
388	    #[test]
389	    fn tolerance_converted_from_stl_units() {
390	        // --stl-units=in means 1e-5 inches = 1e-5 * 25.4 mm
391	        let config = parse(&["input.stl", "--stl-units=in", "--vertex-tolerance=1e-5"]);
392	        let expected = 1e-5 * 25.4;
393	        assert!((config.vertex_tolerance_mm - expected).abs() < 1e-15);
394	    }
395	
396	    #[test]

398	        let s = Stage(2, 3);

400	        assert!(s.at_least(2, 1));
401	        assert!(s.at_least(2, 3));
402	        assert!(!s.at_least(2, 4));
403	        assert!(!s.at_least(3, 1));
404	
405	        // Stage(2, 0) means all of stage 2 = (2, 6)
406	        let s2 = Stage(2, 0);
407	        assert!(s2.at_least(2, 6));
408	        assert!(!s2.at_least(3, 1));
409	    }
410

415	    }
416

421	            // Just verify it parses without error

45	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
46	// Constants

48	
49	/// Lower bound on cross product magnitude |n₁ × n₂| for a valid cylinder/sphere
50	/// seed pair. Since |n₁ × n₂| = sin(θ) for unit normals, this corresponds to a
51	/// minimum dihedral angle of arcsin(0.01) ≈ 0.57° (~0.6°). Seed pairs closer
52	/// than this are too nearly parallel for numerically stable axis estimation
53	/// (cylinders) or sphere fitting (spheres). Together with \`angular_tol\` (the
54	/// upper bound, typically 17.5°), this defines the valid seed-pair window
55	/// [~0.6°, angular_tol].
56	pub(crate) const MIN_CROSS_THRESHOLD: f64 = 0.01;
57	
58	/// Fast-reject multiplier for BFS vertex-distance checks. When a candidate face
59	/// has a vertex farther than \`REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER × vertex_tol\` from the
60	/// current fitted surface, the distance is too large for a re-fit to absorb and
61	/// the face is rejected immediately without attempting re-fitting.
62	/// Set to 10.0 to allow aggressive refitting — the refit itself validates all
63	/// faces, so the only cost of a high multiplier is more refit attempts.
64	pub(crate) const REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER: f64 = 10.0;
65	
66	/// Minimum number of mesh faces required to accept a cylindrical hypothesis.
67	/// Any real CAD tessellation of a cylinder produces at least 3 facets around
68	/// the circumference; this rejects spurious 2-face fits (e.g. adjacent torus
69	/// facets that locally approximate a cylinder).
70	pub(crate) const MIN_CYLINDER_FACES: usize = 3;
71	
72	/// Minimum number of mesh faces required to accept a spherical hypothesis.
73	/// Sphere fitting has 4 degrees of freedom (cx, cy, cz, r), so at least 4
74	/// non-degenerate faces are needed for a well-determined fit. This also rejects
75	/// spurious fits from small patches that are consistent with many surface types.
76	pub(crate) const MIN_SPHERE_FACES: usize = 4;
77	
78	/// Minimum number of mesh faces required to accept a conical hypothesis.
79	/// A cone has 5 degrees of freedom (apex xyz, axis direction 2, half-angle),
80	/// so at least 6 faces are needed for a well-determined and robust fit.
81	pub(crate) const MIN_CONE_FACES: usize = 6;
82	
83	/// Minimum number of mesh faces required to accept a toroidal hypothesis.
84	/// A torus has 7 degrees of freedom (center xyz, axis direction 2, R, r),
85	/// so at least 7 faces are needed for a well-determined fit.
86	pub(crate) const MIN_TORUS_FACES: usize = 7;
87	
88	/// Maximum sphere radius as a multiple of the mesh bounding-box diagonal.
89	/// With solid-angle coverage validation and surface-tolerance validation during
90	/// BFS, this no longer needs to be tight — those checks prevent pathological
91	/// growth. A large value serves as a numerical guardrail against degenerate fits.
92	pub(crate) const MAX_SPHERE_RADIUS_FACTOR: f64 = 1000.0;
93	
94	/// Minimum eigenvalue ratio for solid-angle coverage validation.
95	/// Centroid-to-center direction vectors of a genuine spherical hypothesis span
96	/// 3D, so all eigenvalues of their covariance matrix are substantial.
97	/// Fillet-strip growth produces nearly coplanar directions (λ₃ ≈ 0).
98	pub(crate) const MIN_SPHERE_EIGENVALUE_RATIO: f64 = 0.01;

110	    pub distance: f64,

112	    pub faces: Vec<usize>,
113	    /// Set of mesh vertex indices on this plane.
114	    pub vertices: Vec<usize>,
115	    /// Maximum (positive) distance from any vertex to the plane.
116	    pub error_max: f64,
117	    /// Minimum (most negative) distance from any vertex to the plane.
118	    pub error_min: f64,
119	    /// Sum of absolute vertex-to-plane distances.
120	    pub error_abs_sum: f64,
121	}
122	
123	/// A cylindrical or conical surface hypothesis fitted to a set of mesh faces.
124	/// Cylinders are represented as cones with half_angle == 0.

126	pub struct ConicalHypothesis {
127	    /// The apex of the cone, or a point on the axis for cylinders.

130	    pub axis_direction: [f64; 3],
131	    /// Half-angle of the cone in radians (0 for cylinders).

134	    pub radius_at_apex: f64,
135	    /// Whether the surface normal points away from the axis (convex=true) or toward it (concave=false).
136	    pub convex: bool,
137	    /// Set of mesh face indices that fit this hypothesis.

139	    /// Set of mesh vertex indices on this surface.
140	    pub vertices: Vec<usize>,
141	    /// Maximum absolute distance from any vertex to the surface.
142	    pub error_max: f64,
143	    /// Maximum absolute distance from any face centroid to the surface.
144	    pub centroid_error_max: f64,
145	    /// Sum of absolute vertex-to-surface distances.

147	}

246	    pub planar_hypotheses: Vec<PlanarHypothesis>,
247	    /// Selected conical/cylindrical hypotheses (after stage 2.6 pruning).
248	    /// Cylinders are represented as cones with half_angle == 0.
249	    pub conical_hypotheses: Vec<ConicalHypothesis>,
250	    /// Selected spherical hypotheses (after stage 2.6 pruning).
251	    pub spherical_hypotheses: Vec<SphericalHypothesis>,
252	    /// Selected toroidal hypotheses (after stage 2.6 pruning).
253	    pub toroidal_hypotheses: Vec<ToroidalHypothesis>,
254	    /// B-spline hypotheses fitted by the stage 2.7 NURBS fallback. These own
255	    /// the actual OCCT surfaces (stage 3 reuses the same objects).
256	    pub bspline_hypotheses: Vec<BSplineHypothesis>,
257	    /// Surfaces selected in stage 2.6 for reconstruction. Each face is
258	    /// covered by exactly one selected surface. Indices point into the
259	    /// (pruned) hypothesis vectors above.
260	    pub selected_surfaces: Vec<SelectedSurface>,
261	}

267	#[derive(Debug)]
268	pub enum Stage2Error {
269	    Compare(Stage2CompareError),
270	}
271	
272	impl Display for Stage2Error {
273	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
274	        match self {
275	            Stage2Error::Compare(e) => write!(f, "stage 2 compare: {e}"),
276	        }
277	    }
278	}
279	
280	impl Error for Stage2Error {}
281	
282	impl From<Stage2CompareError> for Stage2Error {
283	    fn from(e: Stage2CompareError) -> Self {
284	        Stage2Error::Compare(e)
285	    }
286	}
287	
288	#[derive(Debug)]
289	pub struct Stage2CompareError {
290	    pub hypothesis_type: &'static str,
291	    pub hypothesis_index: usize,
292	    pub max_distance: f64,
293	    pub tolerance: f64,
294	}
295	
296	impl Display for Stage2CompareError {
297	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
298	        write!(
299	            f,
300	            "{} hypothesis {} projected centroid is {:.6e} mm from nearest STEP surface (tolerance: {:.6e} mm)",
301	            self.hypothesis_type, self.hypothesis_index, self.max_distance, self.tolerance
302	        )
303	    }
304	}
305	
306	impl Error for Stage2CompareError {}

308	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
309	// Shared math utilities
310	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
311	
312	/// Compute the area of a triangular mesh face.
313	pub(crate) fn face_area(face: &MeshFace, vertices: &[MeshVertex]) -> f64 {
314	    let v0 = &vertices[face.vertex_indices[0]];
315	    let v1 = &vertices[face.vertex_indices[1]];
316	    let v2 = &vertices[face.vertex_indices[2]];
317	    let ax = v1.x - v0.x;
318	    let ay = v1.y - v0.y;
319	    let az = v1.z - v0.z;
320	    let bx = v2.x - v0.x;
321	    let by = v2.y - v0.y;
322	    let bz = v2.z - v0.z;
323	    let cx = ay * bz - az * by;
324	    let cy = az * bx - ax * bz;
325	    let cz = ax * by - ay * bx;
326	    let mut area = 0.5 * (cx * cx + cy * cy + cz * cz).sqrt();
327	    if face.vertex_count == 4 {
328	        let v3 = &vertices[face.vertex_indices[3]];
329	        let dx = v3.x - v0.x;
330	        let dy = v3.y - v0.y;
331	        let dz = v3.z - v0.z;
332	        let ex = by * dz - bz * dy;
333	        let ey = bz * dx - bx * dz;
334	        let ez = bx * dy - by * dx;
335	        area += 0.5 * (ex * ex + ey * ey + ez * ez).sqrt();
336	    }
337	    area
338	}
339	
340	/// Compute the face centroid for a mesh face.
341	pub(crate) fn face_centroid(face: &MeshFace, vertices: &[MeshVertex]) -> [f64; 3] {
342	    let n = face.vertex_count as usize;
343	    let mut cx = 0.0_f64;
344	    let mut cy = 0.0_f64;
345	    let mut cz = 0.0_f64;
346	    for vi_idx in 0..n {
347	        let v = &vertices[face.vertex_indices[vi_idx]];
348	        cx += v.x;
349	        cy += v.y;
350	        cz += v.z;
351	    }
352	    let inv_n = 1.0 / n as f64;
353	    [cx * inv_n, cy * inv_n, cz * inv_n]
354	}
355	
356	/// Normalize a 3D vector in place, return its length.
357	pub(crate) fn normalize3(v: &mut [f64; 3]) -> f64 {
358	    let len = (v[0] * v[0] + v[1] * v[1] + v[2] * v[2]).sqrt();
359	    if len > 0.0 {
360	        v[0] /= len;
361	        v[1] /= len;
362	        v[2] /= len;
363	    }
364	    len
365	}
366	
367	/// Cross product of two 3D vectors.
368	pub(crate) fn cross3(a: &[f64; 3], b: &[f64; 3]) -> [f64; 3] {
369	    [
370	        a[1] * b[2] - a[2] * b[1],
371	        a[2] * b[0] - a[0] * b[2],
372	        a[0] * b[1] - a[1] * b[0],
373	    ]
374	}
375	
376	/// Dot product of two 3D vectors.
377	pub(crate) fn dot3(a: &[f64; 3], b: &[f64; 3]) -> f64 {
378	    a[0] * b[0] + a[1] * b[1] + a[2] * b[2]
379	}
380	
381	/// Compute eigenvalues of a 3x3 symmetric matrix stored as upper triangle
382	/// [m00, m01, m02, m11, m12, m22]. Returns eigenvalues in unspecified order.
383	pub(crate) fn eigenvalues_3x3_symmetric(m: &[f64; 6]) -> [f64; 3] {
384	    let a00 = m[0]; let a01 = m[1]; let a02 = m[2];
385	    let a11 = m[3]; let a12 = m[4]; let a22 = m[5];
386	
387	    let c2 = a00 + a11 + a22;
388	    let c1 = a00 * a11 - a01 * a01 + a00 * a22 - a02 * a02 + a11 * a22 - a12 * a12;
389	    let c0 = a00 * (a11 * a22 - a12 * a12)
390	            - a01 * (a01 * a22 - a12 * a02)
391	            + a02 * (a01 * a12 - a11 * a02);
392	
393	    let p = c1 - c2 * c2 / 3.0;
394	    let q = -2.0 * c2 * c2 * c2 / 27.0 + c1 * c2 / 3.0 - c0;
395	
396	    if p.abs() < 1e-30 {
397	        let ev = c2 / 3.0;
398	        return [ev, ev, ev];
399	    }
400	
401	    let neg_p_3 = (-p / 3.0).max(0.0);
402	    let r = neg_p_3.sqrt();
403	    let cos_arg = (-q / (2.0 * neg_p_3 * r)).clamp(-1.0, 1.0);
404	    let theta = cos_arg.acos() / 3.0;
405	    let shift = c2 / 3.0;
406	    let two_pi_3 = 2.0 * std::f64::consts::PI / 3.0;
407	    [2.0 * r * theta.cos() + shift,
408	     2.0 * r * (theta - two_pi_3).cos() + shift,
409	     2.0 * r * (theta - 2.0 * two_pi_3).cos() + shift]
410	}
411	
412	/// Compute the eigenvector of a 3x3 symmetric matrix for a given eigenvalue.
413	/// Uses cross products of (M - λI) rows to find the null space.
414	fn eigenvector_for_eigenvalue(m: &[f64; 6], lambda: f64) -> [f64; 3] {
415	    let b00 = m[0] - lambda;
416	    let b11 = m[3] - lambda;
417	    let b22 = m[5] - lambda;
418	
419	    let row0 = [b00, m[1], m[2]];
420	    let row1 = [m[1], b11, m[4]];
421	    let row2 = [m[2], m[4], b22];
422	
423	    let candidates = [
424	        cross3(&row0, &row1),
425	        cross3(&row0, &row2),
426	        cross3(&row1, &row2),
427	    ];
428	
429	    let mut best_idx = 0;
430	    let mut best_len_sq = 0.0;
431	    for (i, c) in candidates.iter().enumerate() {
432	        let len_sq = c[0] * c[0] + c[1] * c[1] + c[2] * c[2];
433	        if len_sq > best_len_sq {
434	            best_len_sq = len_sq;
435	            best_idx = i;
436	        }
437	    }
438	
439	    let mut ev = candidates[best_idx];
440	    let len = normalize3(&mut ev);
441	    if len < 1e-15 {
442	        return [1.0, 0.0, 0.0];
443	    }
444	    ev
445	}
446	
447	/// Returns the eigenvector corresponding to the smallest eigenvalue.
448	pub(crate) fn smallest_eigenvector_3x3(m: &[f64; 6]) -> [f64; 3] {
449	    let eigenvalues = eigenvalues_3x3_symmetric(m);
450	    let (min_idx, _) = eigenvalues.iter().enumerate()
451	        .min_by(|a, b| a.1.partial_cmp(b.1).unwrap())
452	        .unwrap();
453	    eigenvector_for_eigenvalue(m, eigenvalues[min_idx])
454	}
455	
456	/// Given a 3x3 symmetric matrix, return the eigenvector for the largest eigenvalue.
457	pub(crate) fn largest_eigenvector_3x3(m: &[f64; 6]) -> [f64; 3] {
458	    let eigenvalues = eigenvalues_3x3_symmetric(m);
459	    let (max_idx, _) = eigenvalues.iter().enumerate()
460	        .max_by(|a, b| a.1.partial_cmp(b.1).unwrap())
461	        .unwrap();
462	    eigenvector_for_eigenvalue(m, eigenvalues[max_idx])
463	}
464	
465	/// Build the area-weighted normal covariance matrix M = Σ wᵢ nᵢ nᵢᵀ.
466	/// Returns the upper triangle [m00, m01, m02, m11, m12, m22].
467	pub(crate) fn build_normal_covariance(
468	    face_indices: &[usize],
469	    faces: &[MeshFace],
470	    vertices: &[MeshVertex],
471	) -> [f64; 6] {
472	    let mut m = [0.0_f64; 6];
473	    for &fi in face_indices {
474	        let face = &faces[fi];
475	        let n = face.normal.unwrap();
476	        let w = face_area(face, vertices);
477	        m[0] += w * n[0] * n[0];
478	        m[1] += w * n[0] * n[1];
479	        m[2] += w * n[0] * n[2];
480	        m[3] += w * n[1] * n[1];
481	        m[4] += w * n[1] * n[2];
482	        m[5] += w * n[2] * n[2];
483	    }
484	    m
485	}
486	
487	/// Build an orthonormal basis (u, w) perpendicular to a unit direction \`d\`.
488	pub(crate) fn perpendicular_basis(d: &[f64; 3]) -> ([f64; 3], [f64; 3]) {
489	    let (ax, ay, az) = (d[0].abs(), d[1].abs(), d[2].abs());
490	    let perp = if ax <= ay && ax <= az {
491	        [1.0, 0.0, 0.0]
492	    } else if ay <= az {
493	        [0.0, 1.0, 0.0]
494	    } else {
495	        [0.0, 0.0, 1.0]
496	    };
497	    let mut u = cross3(d, &perp);
498	    normalize3(&mut u);
499	    let w = cross3(d, &u);
500	    (u, w)
501	}
502	
503	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
504	// BFS visualization helpers
505	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
506	
507	/// Compute the centroid of a mesh face (as f32 for viz).
508	pub(crate) fn viz_face_centroid(face_idx: usize, mesh: &ConnectedMesh) -> [f32; 3] {
509	    let face = &mesh.faces[face_idx];
510	    let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
511	    let mut cx = 0.0f64;
512	    let mut cy = 0.0f64;
513	    let mut cz = 0.0f64;
514	    for i in 0..vc {
515	        let v = &mesh.vertices[face.vertex_indices[i]];
516	        cx += v.x;
517	        cy += v.y;
518	        cz += v.z;
519	    }
520	    [cx as f32 / vc as f32, cy as f32 / vc as f32, cz as f32 / vc as f32]
521	}
522	
523	/// Return the normal of a mesh face as f32 (for viz camera orientation).
524	pub(crate) fn viz_face_normal(face_idx: usize, mesh: &ConnectedMesh) -> Option<[f32; 3]> {
525	    mesh.faces[face_idx].normal.map(|n| [n[0] as f32, n[1] as f32, n[2] as f32])
526	}
527	
528	/// Create a CylinderOverlay centered on the axial extent of the given faces.
529	pub(crate) fn centered_cylinder_overlay(
530	    origin: [f64; 3], direction: [f64; 3], radius: f64,
531	    face_list: &[usize], mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
532	    color: [f32; 4],
533	) -> viz::CylinderOverlay {
534	    // Compute full mesh bounding box extent for cylinder length
535	    let mut bb_min = [f64::INFINITY; 3];
536	    let mut bb_max = [f64::NEG_INFINITY; 3];
537	    for v in &mesh.vertices {
538	        let coords = [v.x, v.y, v.z];
539	        for i in 0..3 {
540	            bb_min[i] = bb_min[i].min(coords[i]);
541	            bb_max[i] = bb_max[i].max(coords[i]);
542	        }
543	    }
544	    let extent = ((bb_max[0] - bb_min[0]).powi(2)
545	        + (bb_max[1] - bb_min[1]).powi(2)
546	        + (bb_max[2] - bb_min[2]).powi(2))
547	    .sqrt();
548	
549	    // Center on face list's axial extent
550	    let mut t_min = f64::INFINITY;
551	    let mut t_max = f64::NEG_INFINITY;
552	    for &fi in face_list {
553	        let c = face_centroid(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices);
554	        let d = [c[0] - origin[0], c[1] - origin[1], c[2] - origin[2]];
555	        let t = dot3(&d, &direction);
556	        t_min = t_min.min(t);
557	        t_max = t_max.max(t);
558	    }
559	    let t_mid = (t_min + t_max) * 0.5;
560	    let half_len = extent;
561	    let centered_origin = [
562	        origin[0] + direction[0] * t_mid,
563	        origin[1] + direction[1] * t_mid,
564	        origin[2] + direction[2] * t_mid,
565	    ];
566	    viz::CylinderOverlay {
567	        origin: centered_origin,
568	        direction,
569	        radius,
570	        half_length: half_len,
571	        color,
572	    }
573	}
574	
575	/// Create a ConeOverlay spanning the axial extent of the given faces (with padding).
576	/// Ensures the cone is at least 2x the maximum model dimension in length.
577	pub(crate) fn centered_cone_overlay(
578	    apex: [f64; 3], direction: [f64; 3], half_angle: f64,
579	    face_list: &[usize], mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
580	    color: [f32; 4],
581	) -> viz::ConeOverlay {
582	    // Compute axial extent of face centroids relative to apex
583	    let mut t_min = f64::INFINITY;
584	    let mut t_max = f64::NEG_INFINITY;
585	    for &fi in face_list {
586	        let c = face_centroid(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices);
587	        let d = [c[0] - apex[0], c[1] - apex[1], c[2] - apex[2]];
588	        let t = dot3(&d, &direction);
589	        t_min = t_min.min(t);
590	        t_max = t_max.max(t);
591	    }
592	    // Ensure the cone is at least 2x the maximum model dimension in length
593	    let bb_diag = bounding_box_diagonal(&mesh.vertices);
594	    let min_half_length = bb_diag; // 2x max dimension means full length = 2 * bb_diag, so half_length = bb_diag
595	    let center_t = (t_min + t_max) * 0.5;
596	    let half_span = ((t_max - t_min) * 0.5).max(min_half_length);
597	    // Pad by 20% for visual clarity
598	    let pad = half_span * 0.2;
599	    viz::ConeOverlay {
600	        apex,
601	        direction,
602	        half_angle,
603	        t_min: center_t - half_span - pad,
604	        t_max: center_t + half_span + pad,
605	        color,
606	    }
607	}
608	
609	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
610	// Stage 2.6: Select surfaces for reconstruction
611	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
612	
613	/// Greedy area-based surface selection.
614	///
615	/// Selects hypotheses greedily by total mesh face area (largest first).
616	/// This naturally resolves conflicts: real surface hypotheses cover many faces
617	/// with large total area, while bogus hypotheses cover minimal area and lose.
618	///
619	/// After selection, updates hypothesis face/vertex lists to reflect only the
620	/// faces actually assigned to each selected surface.
621	fn select_surfaces(
622	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
623	    planar_hypotheses: &mut [PlanarHypothesis],
624	    conical_hypotheses: &mut [ConicalHypothesis],
625	    spherical_hypotheses: &mut [SphericalHypothesis],
626	    toroidal_hypotheses: &mut [ToroidalHypothesis],
627	    viz: Option<&VizSender>,
628	    verbosity: u8,
629	) -> Vec<SelectedSurface> {
630	    let num_faces = mesh.faces.len();
631	
632	    // Step 1: Compute geometric area of each mesh face.
633	    let face_areas: Vec<f64> = (0..num_faces)
634	        .map(|fi| face_area(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices))
635	        .collect();
636	
637	    // Step 2: Build candidate list from multi-face hypotheses.
638	    struct Candidate {
639	        surface_type: u8, // 0=planar, 1=conical(cylinder), 2=spherical, 3=conical(cone), 4=toroidal
640	        hypothesis_index: usize,
641	        faces: Vec<usize>,
642	    }
643	
644	    let mut candidates: Vec<Candidate> = Vec::new();
645	
646	    for (i, hyp) in planar_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
647	        if hyp.faces.len() > 1 {
648	            candidates.push(Candidate {
649	                surface_type: 0,
650	                hypothesis_index: i,
651	                faces: hyp.faces.clone(),
652	            });
653	        }
654	    }
655	    for (i, hyp) in conical_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
656	        let st = if hyp.is_cylinder() { 1 } else { 3 };
657	        candidates.push(Candidate {
658	            surface_type: st,
659	            hypothesis_index: i,
660	            faces: hyp.faces.clone(),
661	        });
662	    }
663	    for (i, hyp) in spherical_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
664	        candidates.push(Candidate {
665	            surface_type: 2,
666	            hypothesis_index: i,
667	            faces: hyp.faces.clone(),
668	        });
669	    }
670	    for (i, hyp) in toroidal_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
671	        candidates.push(Candidate {
672	            surface_type: 4,
673	            hypothesis_index: i,
674	            faces: hyp.faces.clone(),
675	        });
676	    }
677	
678	    // Step 3: Greedy loop — select candidate with largest remaining area.
679	    let mut assigned = vec![false; num_faces];
680	    let mut selected: Vec<SelectedSurface> = Vec::new();
681	
682	    loop {
683	        let mut best_ci = None;
684	        let mut best_area = 0.0_f64;
685	
686	        for (ci, cand) in candidates.iter().enumerate() {
687	            let area: f64 = cand.faces.iter()
688	                .filter(|fi| !assigned[**fi])
689	                .map(|fi| face_areas[*fi])
690	                .sum();
691	            if area > best_area {
692	                best_area = area;
693	                best_ci = Some(ci);
694	            }
695	        }
696	
697	        let Some(ci) = best_ci else { break };
698	
699	        // Collect still-unassigned faces for the winning candidate.
700	        let sel_faces: Vec<usize> = candidates[ci].faces.iter()
701	            .filter(|fi| !assigned[**fi])
702	            .copied()
703	            .collect();
704	
705	
706	        // Viz: show selected hypothesis and competing hypotheses
707	        if let Some(viz_sender) = viz {
708	            let mut overlay = viz::VizOverlay::new();
709	
710	            // Already-assigned faces in dark gray
711	            let assigned_faces: Vec<usize> = (0..num_faces)
712	                .filter(|&fi| assigned[fi])
713	                .collect();
714	            if !assigned_faces.is_empty() {
715	                overlay.face_highlights.push(viz::FaceHighlight {
716	                    face_indices: assigned_faces,
717	                    color: [0.3, 0.3, 0.3, 1.0],
718	                });
719	            }
720	
721	            // Faces being assigned in green
722	            overlay.face_highlights.push(viz::FaceHighlight {
723	                face_indices: sel_faces.clone(),
724	                color: [0.0, 0.8, 0.0, 1.0],
725	            });
726	
727	            // Find competing hypotheses that involve any of the sel_faces
728	            let sel_face_set: HashSet<usize> = sel_faces.iter().copied().collect();
729	            for (oi, other) in candidates.iter().enumerate() {
730	                if oi == ci {
731	                    continue;
732	                }
733	                let competing_faces: Vec<usize> = other.faces.iter()
734	                    .filter(|fi| !assigned[**fi] && sel_face_set.contains(fi))
735	                    .copied()
736	                    .collect();
737	                if !competing_faces.is_empty() {
738	                    overlay.face_highlights.push(viz::FaceHighlight {
739	                        face_indices: competing_faces,
740	                        color: [0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 0.25],
741	                    });
742	                    // Show the competing hypothesis surface overlay
743	                    match other.surface_type {
744	                        1 => {
745	                            let hyp = &conical_hypotheses[other.hypothesis_index];
746	                            overlay.cylinders.push(centered_cylinder_overlay(
747	                                hyp.apex, hyp.axis_direction, hyp.radius_at_apex,
748	                                &other.faces, mesh,
749	                                [0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 0.25],
750	                            ));
751	                        }
752	                        2 => {
753	                            let hyp = &spherical_hypotheses[other.hypothesis_index];
754	                            overlay.spheres.push(viz::SphereOverlay {
755	                                center: hyp.center,
756	                                radius: hyp.radius,
757	                                color: [0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 0.25],
758	                            });
759	                        }
760	                        3 => {
761	                            let hyp = &conical_hypotheses[other.hypothesis_index];
762	                            overlay.cones.push(centered_cone_overlay(
763	                                hyp.apex, hyp.axis_direction, hyp.half_angle,
764	                                &other.faces, mesh,
765	                                [0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 0.25],
766	                            ));
767	                        }
768	                        4 => {
769	                            let hyp = &toroidal_hypotheses[other.hypothesis_index];
770	                            overlay.tori.push(viz::TorusOverlay {
771	                                center: hyp.center,
772	                                direction: hyp.axis_direction,
773	                                major_radius: hyp.major_radius,
774	                                minor_radius: hyp.minor_radius,
775	                                color: [0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 0.25],
776	                            });
777	                        }
778	                        _ => {}
779	                    }
780	                }
781	            }
782	
783	            // Show the winning hypothesis surface overlay in green
784	            let type_name = match candidates[ci].surface_type {
785	                0 => {
786	                    // Planar — faces are sufficient
787	                    "planar"
788	                }
789	                1 => {

930	/// Distance from a mesh vertex to a selected surface's fitted geometry.
931	fn vertex_distance_to_selected(
932	    v: &MeshVertex,
933	    surface: &SelectedSurface,
934	    planar_hypotheses: &[PlanarHypothesis],
935	    conical_hypotheses: &[ConicalHypothesis],
936	    spherical_hypotheses: &[SphericalHypothesis],
937	    toroidal_hypotheses: &[ToroidalHypothesis],
938	    bspline_hypotheses: &[BSplineHypothesis],
939	) -> f64 {
940	    match surface {
941	        SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => {
942	            bspline_hypotheses[*i].distance(&[v.x, v.y, v.z])
943	        }
944	        SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => {
945	            let h = &planar_hypotheses[*i];
946	            (h.normal[0] * v.x + h.normal[1] * v.y + h.normal[2] * v.z - h.distance).abs()
947	        }
948	        SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => {
949	            let h = &conical_hypotheses[*i];
950	            if h.is_cylinder() {
951	                cylindrical::vertex_to_cylinder_distance(
952	                    v, &h.apex, &h.axis_direction, h.radius_at_apex,
953	                ).abs()
954	            } else {
955	                conical::vertex_to_cone_distance(
956	                    v, &h.apex, &h.axis_direction, h.half_angle,
957	                ).abs()
958	            }
959	        }
960	        SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => {
961	            let h = &spherical_hypotheses[*i];
962	            spherical::vertex_to_sphere_distance(v, &h.center, h.radius).abs()
963	        }
964	        SelectedSurface::Toroidal(i) => {
965	            let h = &toroidal_hypotheses[*i];
966	            toroidal::vertex_to_torus_distance(
967	                v, &h.center, &h.axis_direction, h.major_radius, h.minor_radius,
968	            ).abs()
969	        }
970	    }
971	}
972	
973	/// Refine surface assignments at region boundaries (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN §2.6
974	/// "Surface refitting"). BFS greedily absorbs border faces that fit within
975	/// tolerance but belong more naturally to the adjacent surface — e.g. a face
976	/// one row past a fillet junction absorbed by the plane. The misassignment
977	/// shifts the mesh region boundary by a row, which shifts reconstructed edge
978	/// boundary vertices and corner vertices off the true feature curves.
979	///
980	/// For each face on a region boundary, compare the max vertex distance to
981	/// its own surface against each adjacent selected surface, and move the face
982	/// when an adjacent surface fits at least 2× better. The margin prevents
983	/// oscillation for true junction faces (whose vertices lie on both surfaces
984	/// equally). Iterates to a fixpoint (boundaries only ever move locally).
985	/// Hypothesis geometry is NOT re-fitted: the fits were validated during BFS;
986	/// only the region map (face membership) changes.
987	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
988	fn refine_boundary_assignments(
989	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
990	    selected_surfaces: &mut Vec<SelectedSurface>,
991	    planar_hypotheses: &mut [PlanarHypothesis],
992	    conical_hypotheses: &mut [ConicalHypothesis],
993	    spherical_hypotheses: &mut [SphericalHypothesis],
994	    toroidal_hypotheses: &mut [ToroidalHypothesis],
995	    bspline_hypotheses: &mut [BSplineHypothesis],
996	    config: &Config,
997	) {
998	    let num_faces = mesh.faces.len();
999	
1000	    let face_max_dist = |fi: usize,
1001	                         surface: &SelectedSurface,
1002	                         planar: &[PlanarHypothesis],
1003	                         conical: &[ConicalHypothesis],
1004	                         spherical: &[SphericalHypothesis],
1005	                         toroidal: &[ToroidalHypothesis],
1006	                         bspline: &[BSplineHypothesis]|
1007	     -> f64 {
1008	        let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
1009	        let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
1010	        face.vertex_indices[..vc]
1011	            .iter()
1012	            .map(|&vi| {
1013	                vertex_distance_to_selected(
1014	                    &mesh.vertices[vi], surface, planar, conical, spherical, toroidal,
1015	                    bspline,
1016	                )
1017	            })
1018	            .fold(0.0_f64, f64::max)
1019	    };
1020	
1021	    let mut total_moved = 0_usize;
1022	    for _iteration in 0..10 {
1023	        // Face → selected surface index map.
1024	        let mut face_to_surface = vec![usize::MAX; num_faces];
1025	        for (si, s) in selected_surfaces.iter().enumerate() {
1026	            let faces = match s {
1027	                SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => &planar_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1028	                SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => &conical_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1029	                SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => &spherical_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1030	                SelectedSurface::Toroidal(i) => &toroidal_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1031	                SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => &bspline_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1032	            };
1033	            for &fi in faces {
1034	                face_to_surface[fi] = si;
1035	            }
1036	        }
1037	
1038	        // Plan moves: (face, from surface, to surface).
1039	        let mut moves: Vec<(usize, usize, usize)> = Vec::new();
1040	        for fi in 0..num_faces {
1041	            let si = face_to_surface[fi];
1042	            if si == usize::MAX {
1043	                continue;
1044	            }
1045	            let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
1046	            let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
1047	            // B-spline surfaces are not move targets (same domain-extension
1048	            // hazard as in absorption: the fit doesn't vouch for faces
1049	            // outside the region it was built from).
1050	            let mut candidates: Vec<usize> = face.neighbors[..vc]
1051	                .iter()
1052	                .filter(|&&ni| ni >= 0)
1053	                .map(|&ni| face_to_surface[ni as usize])
1054	                .filter(|&sj| {
1055	                    sj != usize::MAX
1056	                        && sj != si
1057	                        && !matches!(selected_surfaces[sj], SelectedSurface::BSpline(_))
1058	                })
1059	                .collect();
1060	            if candidates.is_empty() {
1061	                continue;
1062	            }
1063	            candidates.sort_unstable();
1064	            candidates.dedup();
1065	
1066	            let d_own = face_max_dist(
1067	                fi, &selected_surfaces[si],
1068	                planar_hypotheses, conical_hypotheses,
1069	                spherical_hypotheses, toroidal_hypotheses, bspline_hypotheses,
1070	            );
1071	            let mut best: Option<(f64, usize)> = None;
1072	            for &sj in &candidates {
1073	                let d = face_max_dist(
1074	                    fi, &selected_surfaces[sj],
1075	                    planar_hypotheses, conical_hypotheses,
1076	                    spherical_hypotheses, toroidal_hypotheses, bspline_hypotheses,
1077	                );
1078	                if best.as_ref().is_none_or(|(bd, _)| d < *bd) {
1079	                    best = Some((d, sj));
1080	                }
1081	            }
1082	            if let Some((d_best, sj)) = best {
1083	                if d_best < d_own * 0.5 {
1084	                    moves.push((fi, si, sj));
1085	                }
1086	            }
1087	        }
1088	
1089	        if moves.is_empty() {
1090	            break;
1091	        }
1092	        total_moved += moves.len();
1093	
1094	        // Apply moves to hypothesis face lists.
1095	        let mut remove_from = |s: &SelectedSurface, fi: usize| {
1096	            let faces: &mut Vec<usize> = match s {
1097	                SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => &mut planar_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1098	                SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => &mut conical_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1099	                SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => &mut spherical_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1100	                SelectedSurface::Toroidal(i) => &mut toroidal_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1101	                SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => &mut bspline_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1102	            };
1103	            faces.retain(|&f| f != fi);
1104	        };
1105	        for &(fi, si, _) in &moves {
1106	            let s = selected_surfaces[si];
1107	            remove_from(&s, fi);
1108	        }
1109	        let mut add_to = |s: &SelectedSurface, fi: usize| {
1110	            let faces: &mut Vec<usize> = match s {
1111	                SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => &mut planar_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1112	                SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => &mut conical_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1113	                SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => &mut spherical_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1114	                SelectedSurface::Toroidal(i) => &mut toroidal_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1115	                SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => &mut bspline_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1116	            };
1117	            faces.push(fi);
1118	        };
1119	        for &(fi, _, sj) in &moves {
1120	            let s = selected_surfaces[sj];
1121	            add_to(&s, fi);
1122	        }
1123	
1124	        if config.verbosity >= 2 {
1125	            eprintln!("  Boundary refinement: moved {} face(s)", moves.len());
1126	        }
1127	    }
1128	
1129	    if total_moved > 0 {
1130	        // Rebuild vertex lists of all hypotheses from their face lists, and
1131	        // keep face lists sorted for determinism.
1132	        let rebuild = |faces: &mut Vec<usize>, vertices_out: &mut Vec<usize>| {
1133	            faces.sort_unstable();
1134	            let mut verts: Vec<usize> = Vec::new();
1135	            for &fi in faces.iter() {
1136	                let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
1137	                let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
1138	                verts.extend_from_slice(&face.vertex_indices[..vc]);
1139	            }
1140	            verts.sort_unstable();
1141	            verts.dedup();
1142	            *vertices_out = verts;
1143	        };
1144	        for s in selected_surfaces.iter() {
1145	            match s {
1146	                SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => {
1147	                    let h = &mut planar_hypotheses[*i];
1148	                    let mut f = std::mem::take(&mut h.faces);
1149	                    rebuild(&mut f, &mut h.vertices);
1150	                    h.faces = f;
1151	                }
1152	                SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => {
1153	                    let h = &mut conical_hypotheses[*i];
1154	                    let mut f = std::mem::take(&mut h.faces);
1155	                    rebuild(&mut f, &mut h.vertices);
1156	                    h.faces = f;
1157	                }
1158	                SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => {
1159	                    let h = &mut spherical_hypotheses[*i];
1160	                    let mut f = std::mem::take(&mut h.faces);
1161	                    rebuild(&mut f, &mut h.vertices);
1162	                    h.faces = f;
1163	                }
1164	                SelectedSurface::Toroidal(i) => {
1165	                    let h = &mut toroidal_hypotheses[*i];
1166	                    let mut f = std::mem::take(&mut h.faces);
1167	                    rebuild(&mut f, &mut h.vertices);
1168	                    h.faces = f;
1169	                }
1170	                SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => {
1171	                    let h = &mut bspline_hypotheses[*i];
1172	                    let mut f = std::mem::take(&mut h.faces);
1173	                    rebuild(&mut f, &mut h.vertices);
1174	                    h.faces = f;
1175	                }
1176	            }
1177	        }
1178	        // Drop selected surfaces that lost all their faces.
1179	        selected_surfaces.retain(|s| {
1180	            let faces = match s {
1181	                SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => &planar_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1182	                SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => &conical_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1183	                SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => &spherical_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1184	                SelectedSurface::Toroidal(i) => &toroidal_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1185	                SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => &bspline_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1186	            };
1187	            !faces.is_empty()
1188	        });
1189	        if config.verbosity >= 1 {
1190	            eprintln!(
1191	                "  Boundary refinement: {} face(s) reassigned to better-fitting adjacent surfaces",
1192	                total_moved,
1193	            );
1194	        }
1195	    }
1196	}

1198	/// Merge redundant/orphan selected surfaces into larger adjacent ones.
1199	///
1200	/// Two situations produce selected surfaces that duplicate a neighbor's
1201	/// geometry rather than describing a real model feature:
1202	///
1203	/// 1. **Spurious sub-fits**: a narrow band of faces on a cone/sphere can fit
1204	///    an "exact" cylinder (e.g. 3 azimuthal strips of a shallow cone have
1205	///    nearly coplanar normals) and get committed before the real surface's
1206	///    BFS reaches those faces. All of the band's vertices lie exactly on the
1207	///    real surface.
1208	/// 2. **Tangent-junction bridge facets**: a mesh facet spanning a tangent
1209	///    junction (e.g. cone–sphere) has vertices on both sides and can't be
1210	///    absorbed by either surface at vertex tolerance; it becomes an orphan
1211	///    single-face planar surface. Its chordal deviation from either surface
1212	///    is bounded by the tessellation's surface tolerance.
1213	///
1214	/// Both create fake edges/vertices that break stage 3 reconstruction. This
1215	/// pass merges a surface S into an adjacent, larger-area surface T when all
1216	/// of S's vertices lie within tolerance of T's geometry: a tight tolerance
1217	/// (10× vertex tolerance) for multi-face S (case 1: exact overlap), and the
1218	/// looser surface tolerance for S with ≤ 2 faces (case 2: chordal sag).
1219	/// The loose rule additionally requires T to have > 2 faces (a real
1220	/// surface): on a smooth freeform region every single-face strip lies
1221	/// within chordal tolerance of its neighbor, and orphan-to-orphan chaining
1222	/// would dissolve the regions the stage 2.7 NURBS fallback must gather.
1223	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]

1228	    conical_hypotheses: &mut [ConicalHypothesis],
1229	    spherical_hypotheses: &mut [SphericalHypothesis],
1230	    toroidal_hypotheses: &mut [ToroidalHypothesis],
1231	    bspline_hypotheses: &mut [BSplineHypothesis],
1232	    config: &Config,
1233	) {

1246	            SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => bspline[*i].faces.clone(),
1247	        }

1255	            for fi in surface_faces(

2374	            vertex_indices: [a, b, c, 0],

2381	        }

2384	    /// Build a simple mesh from vertices and triangle indices, then validate.
2385	    fn build_mesh(verts: Vec<MeshVertex>, tris: Vec<[usize; 3]>) -> ConnectedMesh {

2387	        let mut mesh = ConnectedMesh {
2388	            vertices: verts,
2389	            faces,
2390	            stats: Default::default(),
2391	        };
2392	        mesh.validate_and_populate_topology().expect("mesh should be valid");
2393	        mesh
2394	    }

2422	    #[test]
2423	    fn seed_scores_highlight_creases() {
2424	        // Two coplanar quads followed by a 90° crease into two more quads:
2425	        // faces adjacent to the crease must score higher than interior
2426	        // faces, and interior faces of both planes must score ~equally low.
2427	        //
2428	        //  z
2429	        //  |  f3 f2   (vertical plane, x=2)
2430	        //  +--f0 f1-- x  (horizontal plane, z=0)
2431	        let verts = vec![
2432	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(0.0, 0.0, 0.0),
2433	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(1.0, 0.0, 0.0),
2434	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(2.0, 0.0, 0.0),
2435	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(0.0, 1.0, 0.0),
2436	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(1.0, 1.0, 0.0),
2437	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(2.0, 1.0, 0.0),
2438	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(2.0, 0.0, 1.0),
2439	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(2.0, 1.0, 1.0),
2440	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(2.0, 0.0, 2.0),
2441	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(2.0, 1.0, 2.0),
2442	        ];
2443	        // Triangles: horizontal plane f0..f3, vertical plane f4..f7.
2444	        let tris = vec![
2445	            [0, 1, 4], [0, 4, 3], // interior horizontal
2446	            [1, 2, 5], [1, 5, 4], // crease-adjacent horizontal
2447	            [2, 6, 7], [2, 7, 5], // crease-adjacent vertical
2448	            [6, 8, 9], [6, 9, 7], // interior vertical
2449	        ];
2450	        let mesh = build_mesh(verts, tris);
2451	        let (scores, junction) = compute_seed_scores(&mesh);
2452	
2453	        let interior_max = scores[0].max(scores[1]).max(scores[6]).max(scores[7]);
2454	        let crease_min = scores[2].min(scores[3]).min(scores[4]).min(scores[5]);
2455	        assert!(
2456	            crease_min > interior_max,
2457	            "crease faces must outscore interior faces: crease_min={crease_min}, interior_max={interior_max}, scores={scores:?}"
2458	        );
2459	        // The four crease-adjacent faces are junctions; interiors are not.
2460	        assert!(junction[2] && junction[3] && junction[4] && junction[5],
2461	                "crease faces should be flagged as junctions: {junction:?}");
2462	        assert!(!junction[0] && !junction[1] && !junction[6] && !junction[7],
2463	                "interior faces should not be junctions: {junction:?}");
2464	    }

2466	    #[test]
2467	    fn cube_gets_six_planar_hypotheses() {
2468	        let stl_path = format!("{}/tests/manual/cube.stl", env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"));
2469	        let mut mesh =
2470	            stage1::read_connected_mesh_from_stl(&stl_path, VertexWeldOptions { tolerance: 1e-9 })
2471	                .expect("should load");
2472	        mesh.validate_and_populate_topology().expect("should validate");
2473	
2474	        let config = default_bfs_config();
2475	        let (hypotheses, _) = bfs::generate_hypotheses::<PlanarSurface>(&mut mesh, &config, None);
2476	
2477	        // A cube has 6 faces, each with 2 triangles
2478	        assert_eq!(mesh.faces.len(), 12);
2479	
2480	        // Should produce exactly 6 planar hypotheses (one per cube face)

2482	            hypotheses.len(),
2483	            6,

2485	            hypotheses.len(),

2487	        );
2488	
2489	        // Each hypothesis should have exactly 2 faces
2490	        for (i, h) in hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
2491	            assert_eq!(
2492	                h.faces.len(),
2493	                2,
2494	                "hypothesis {i} should have 2 faces, got {}",
2495	                h.faces.len()
2496	            );
2497	        }
2498	
2499	        // Total covered faces should be 12
2500	        let total: usize = hypotheses.iter().map(|h| h.faces.len()).sum();
2501	        assert_eq!(total, 12);
2502	
2503	        // All errors should be essentially zero for a perfect cube
2504	        for h in &hypotheses {
2505	            assert!(
2506	                h.error_max.abs() < 1e-12,
2507	                "error_max should be ~0, got {}",
2508	                h.error_max
2509	            );
2510	            assert!(
2511	                h.error_min.abs() < 1e-12,
2512	                "error_min should be ~0, got {}",
2513	                h.error_min
2514	            );
2515	        }
2516	    }

2518	    #[test]
2519	    fn two_perpendicular_triangles_get_separate_hypotheses() {
2520	        // Two triangles sharing an edge but on perpendicular planes.
2521	        // With the generic BFS (min acceptance = 2), each single triangle
2522	        // produces 0 multi-face hypotheses. The actual pipeline synthesizes
2523	        // single-face planar fallbacks in stage2() after BFS, but this unit
2524	        // test verifies that BFS doesn't incorrectly merge them.
2525	        let verts = vec![
2526	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(0.0, 0.0, 0.0),
2527	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(1.0, 0.0, 0.0),
2528	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(0.0, 1.0, 0.0), // triangle 0: on XY plane
2529	            MeshVertex::from_xyz(0.0, 0.0, 1.0), // triangle 1: on XZ plane
2530	        ];
2531	        let mut mesh = build_mesh(verts, vec![[0, 1, 2], [0, 3, 1]]);
2532	        let config = default_bfs_config();
2533	        let (hypotheses, _) = bfs::generate_hypotheses::<PlanarSurface>(&mut mesh, &config, None);
2534	
2535	        // BFS won't produce multi-face hypotheses for single isolated triangles
2536	        // (min acceptance = 2 faces, but perpendicular faces can't merge).
2537	        // Verify no hypotheses merge the two perpendicular faces.
2538	        assert_eq!(
2539	            hypotheses.len(),
2540	            0,
2541	            "perpendicular triangles should get 0 multi-face hypotheses from BFS"
2542	        );
2543	    }

167	### Stage 2: Surface Fitting
168	
169	**Precision note**: Mesh vertex positions, normals, and all hypothesis parameters use double-precision (\`f64\`) throughout. Surface fitting arithmetic and BFS distance computations are all in double precision.
170	
171	#### Generic BFS Hypothesis Generation
172	
173	Stages 2.1–2.5 share a common algorithm structure for deducing surface hypotheses from the mesh. This section describes the generic algorithm; each stage section below describes how it is parameterized. The Rust implementation uses the \`SurfaceType\` trait in \`bfs.rs\` to share code across surface types.
174	
175	The algorithm operates on a single surface type at a time (planar, conical/cylindrical, spherical, toroidal). For each surface type, the algorithm iterates over all faces that don't yet have a hypothesis of that type, attempting to grow a hypothesis from each face.
176	
177	**Hypothesis data structure:**
178	Each surface type defines a hypothesis struct with type-specific parameters (see per-stage sections) plus common fields:
179	- \`faces: Vec<usize>\` — Mesh face indices in this hypothesis.
180	- \`vertices: Vec<usize>\` — Mesh vertex indices on this surface.
181	- \`error_max: f64\` — Maximum absolute distance from any vertex to the fitted surface.
182	- \`error_abs_sum: f64\` — Sum of absolute vertex-to-surface distances.
183	
184	Each \`MeshFace\` has a hypothesis index field per surface type (e.g., \`planar_hypothesis\`, \`conical_hypothesis\`, \`spherical_hypothesis\`, \`toroidal_hypothesis\`), initialized to \`UNDEDUCED\` (-2) at the start of each stage. Conical and cylindrical surfaces share the \`conical_hypothesis\` field (cylinders have \`half_angle == 0\`).
185	
186	**Algorithm: \`generate_hypotheses<S: SurfaceType>(mesh, config)\` — runs the full hypothesis generation for one surface type:**
187	
188	1. Initialize all hypothesis fields for this surface type to \`UNDEDUCED\`.
189	2. For each face \`fi\` in index order where the face is eligible (hypothesis field == \`UNDEDUCED\`, plus any surface-type-specific eligibility checks):
190	    a. **Seed generation**: call \`S::generate_seeds()\` to find all valid seeds anchored at \`fi\` (see per-stage sections for seed types). For each candidate seed:
191	        - Collect the seed faces' vertices.
192	        - Perform a surface fit from the seed vertices using \`S::fit_surface()\`.
193	        - **Seed vertex tolerance**: check all seed face vertices within \`--vertex-tolerance\` of the fitted surface. Reject seed if any vertex exceeds this.
194	        - **Seed surface tolerance**: check all seed face centroids within \`--surface-tolerance\` of the fitted surface. Reject seed if any fails.
195	        - **Seed angular tolerance**: check dihedral angles between all pairs of adjacent seed faces. Reject seed if any exceeds \`--angular-tolerance\`.
196	        - Determine convexity from the seed faces.
197	    b. **BFS expansion** (per valid seed): using temporary data structures (local face list, vertex set — mesh hypothesis fields are NOT mutated during trials):
198	        - Initialize BFS queue with all seed faces.
199	        - Pop faces from the queue. For each neighbor \`ni\` of the popped face:
200	            - Skip if \`ni\` is not eligible for this surface type (already assigned a hypothesis, or fails surface-type-specific eligibility).
201	            - Skip if \`ni\` is already claimed by this trial.
202	            - **Angular tolerance check**: for each of \`ni\`'s mesh neighbors that is already claimed by this trial, compute the dihedral angle. If any exceeds \`--angular-tolerance\`, skip \`ni\`.
203	            - **Convexity check**: call \`S::convexity_check()\`. If it fails, skip \`ni\`.
204	            - **Surface distance check**: compute distance from each vertex of \`ni\` to the fitted surface via \`S::vertex_distance()\`, and from \`ni\`'s centroid via \`S::centroid_distance()\`.
205	                - If all vertices are within \`--vertex-tolerance\` and centroid within \`--surface-tolerance\`: accept directly.
206	                - If any vertex exceeds \`REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER * vertex_tolerance\` (currently 10.0×) or centroid exceeds \`REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER * surface_tolerance\`: skip \`ni\` — too far for refitting to help.
207	                - Otherwise (between 1× and \`REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER\`×): attempt a **re-fit** using all current trial faces plus \`ni\`. If the re-fit satisfies vertex tolerance and surface tolerance for **all** faces (existing and new), accept the re-fit. Otherwise, revert and skip \`ni\`.
208	            - If accepted: add \`ni\` to the trial's face list, add its vertices to the vertex set, push \`ni\` onto the BFS queue.
209	        - After BFS completes: do a final re-fit from all accumulated faces and vertices.
210	    c. **Acceptance tests** (per completed trial): call \`S::acceptance_test()\` for surface-type-specific post-BFS validation (e.g., angular coverage, solid-angle coverage, minimum face count). If validation fails, discard the trial.
211	    d. **Best candidate selection**: among all accepted trials for this starting face \`fi\`, keep the one with the largest total face area. Only two candidates need to be in memory at a time (current best and trial being evaluated).
212	    e. **Commit**: if a best candidate was found, create a hypothesis and assign it to all of its faces (mutating \`mesh.faces[].{type}_hypothesis\`). Faces assigned here will be skipped by subsequent starting faces.
213	3. After all starting faces have been processed: set any remaining \`UNDEDUCED\` faces to \`NO_HYPOTHESIS\`.
214	
215	**Curvature-guided seed ordering**: Start faces are processed in ascending order of a per-face *curvature-difference score* rather than mesh index order. The score is computed once per mesh (in stage 2, shared by all surface stages):
216	
217	1. **Per-face curvature summary**: for each mesh edge between adjacent faces, the discrete normal curvature along the crossing direction is κ ≈ θ/d (dihedral angle over centroid distance). A face's summary is the maximum κ over its edges — approximately its largest principal curvature.
218	2. **Neighbor difference**: a face's raw score is the maximum |summary difference| against its edge neighbors. Interior faces of any uniform surface (plane, cylinder, cone, sphere, torus) score ≈ 0 — curvature is locally constant even where it is nonzero — while faces at surface transitions score high. Crucially, this includes **tangent junctions** (fillet↔plane, fillet↔cylinder), which are G1-continuous and therefore invisible to dihedral-angle checks, but G2-discontinuous: curvature jumps from 0 to 1/r.
219	3. **Diffusion**: 3 Jacobi passes of neighbor averaging (new = ½·self + ½·mean(neighbors)) spread the junction signal a few hops, so ordering prefers deep-interior faces whose seed neighborhoods (1-ring / fan, radius ≈ 1–2) cannot touch a junction.
220	4. **Junction mask**: separately from the diffused ordering score, faces whose RAW score exceeds 50% of the local curvature magnitude (max of own and neighbors' summaries) are flagged as junction faces. The criterion is local-relative, not mesh-global: sharp creases dominate a global maximum and tangent junctions — the primary target — would fall below any fraction of it.
221	
222	Effect: interior seeds run first and commit large, correctly-fitted hypotheses; junction faces are usually absorbed by BFS growth before they ever become start faces (committed faces do not seed). **Seed gathering exclusion**: in pass 1, junction faces are also excluded from seed neighborhoods (conical fans, spherical 1-rings, toroidal hop-expansion), so seeds stay on one surface; a second pass retries remaining undeduced faces without the filter so surfaces composed entirely of transition faces (one-row fillets) still seed. Toroidal gathering hop-expands (up to 3 hops, targeting ≥ 16 faces) since small fillet tubes have little interior across the tube but extend along the fillet axis. Ordering and masks are deterministic: ties break by face index.
223	
224	**Relation to the older seeding masks (verified by measurement, 2026-07-15)**: the junction mask subsumed the \`multiface_planar\` mask (blob-shaped planar regions excluded from torus seeds) — full suite green and timings unchanged with it removed, so it was retired. It does NOT subsume the \`large_sphere\` mask: sphere interiors have uniform curvature and are never junction-flagged, and every adjacent quad-row band on a sphere lies exactly on some torus, so without the mask torus seeding/BFS tile large spheres with bogus-but-valid fits that only die in selection (correct output, catastrophic cost — pill_fine stage 2: 1.2 s → 429 s; rounded_cube_fine: 4.0 s → 479 s; full suite 3.3 → 18.5 min). The large-sphere mask stays.
225	
226	**Computational cost**: For each starting face, the number of seed trials is typically small (1 for planar/spherical/toroidal, 1–6 for conical/cylindrical). Each trial BFS covers the connected surface region. Since most faces on the same surface will discover the same region, the work is dominated by the first few faces that discover the region before their neighbors get committed. The curvature field costs O(N) plus three O(N) diffusion passes — negligible next to LM fitting.
227	
228	- With the \`--compare\` flag (applied to all surface types):
229	  - For each hypothesis, compute the centroid of each member face.
230	  - Project each centroid onto the fitted surface (nearest point on the surface).
231	  - Measure the distance from the projected point to the nearest surface in the reference STEP file using \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\`.
232	  - If any projected centroid exceeds \`--surface-tolerance\`, report an error.
233	  - Note: centroid-to-surface distance measures both fitting accuracy and tessellation sagitta ($\approx h^2 / 8R$ for curvature radius $R$ and chord length $h$). For typical tessellations this is small relative to \`--surface-tolerance\`.
234	
235	#### 2.1 Deduce planar hypotheses
236	
237	Fit planar hypotheses to all connected sets of coplanar faces. Every face is assigned to exactly one planar hypothesis (including single-face hypotheses for faces on curved surfaces). A planar hypothesis consists of:
238	- \`normal: [f64; 3]\` — Unit normal vector pointing outward from the shell/solid.
239	- \`distance: f64\` — Signed distance from origin to plane along the normal (plane equation: normal · p = distance).
240	- \`error_max, error_min: f64\` — Maximum (positive) and minimum (most negative) signed distance from any vertex to the plane.
241	- \`error_abs_sum: f64\` — Sum of absolute vertex-to-plane distances.
242	
243	Runs the generic BFS hypothesis generation with the following parameterization:
244	
245	**Seed type**: The starting face \`fi\` alone. No additional seed faces are needed — a single face defines a plane.
246	
247	**Surface fit**: Area-weighted normal averaging. Each face's unit normal is weighted by the face's triangle area, then the weighted sum is normalized. The plane distance is the mean of \`normal · vertex\` over all vertices. This gives larger faces more influence on the plane orientation. See [SURFACE_FITTING.md](SURFACE_FITTING.md) for detailed fitting math.
248	
249	**Distance metric**: Signed distance from vertex to plane: \`d = normal · vertex - distance\`. All vertices must satisfy \`|d| ≤ vertex_tolerance\`.
250	
251	Because the vertex tolerance is tight for CAD meshes, the angular tolerance check is redundant for planar hypotheses — any face with all vertices in the tolerance band necessarily has a closely aligned normal. It is still applied for consistency with the generic algorithm.
252	
253	**Acceptance tests**: \`face_count >= 2\`. Single-face planes are not kept as hypotheses. Faces that end up in a single-face group are left as \`NO_HYPOTHESIS\`; stage 2.6 synthesizes single-face planar hypotheses for any faces that received no hypothesis from stages 2.1–2.5.
254	
255	**Execution order note**: Stages execute planar (2.1) → spherical (2.3) →
256	toroidal (2.5) → conical (2.2) → selection (2.6), keeping the historical
257	stage numbering for CLI/viz compatibility. The principle is most-constrained
258	surfaces first: every band of quad rows on a tessellated sphere lies exactly
259	on some cone or cylinder, and any adjacent pair of quad rows on a torus tube
260	likewise lies exactly on some cone — so if the conical stage ran first it
261	would tile spheres and fillets with bogus band hypotheses (slow, and they
262	starve the later stages' seeding). Sphere and torus acceptance tests are
263	selective enough to run early (solid-angle coverage; normal-based tube-span
264	coverage), and cylinders resist bogus torus fits cheaply (tube centers
265	collapse onto the axis; the (h,r) profile is a constant-radius line).
266	Conical seeding skips sphere- and torus-committed faces; conical BFS
```
</details>

```

**After:**
```
    /// Check if a specific stage should be visualized.
    pub fn viz_active(&self, major: u8, minor: u8) -> bool {
        self.viz_stages.iter().any(|s| {
            let (sm, ss) = s.normalized();
            sm == major && ss == minor
        })
    }

    /// Check if a specific sub-stage was named in --skip-stage. Matches the
    /// exact (major, minor) pair as parsed — unlike `viz_active`/`at_least`,
    /// a bare major (minor 0) is not expanded, since the recognition
    /// sub-stages this is meant for (2.2, 2.3, 2.5, 2.7) always need an
    /// explicit minor.
    pub fn skip_stage(&self, major: u8, minor: u8) -> bool {
        self.skip_stages.iter().any(|s| s.0 == major && s.1 == minor)
    }
```

## 🤖💭 Assistant
<!-- msg_011Cd6dpdBZjuzffwDhQJcct -->

> The file header looks like ASCII text "solid Shape0" but when I tried reading it as binary STL format, I got a nonsensical count of 225706 from misinterpreting those ASCII bytes as binary length data. The issue is that many binary STL files actually start with "solid ..." text in their 80-byte header for backwards compatibility, which makes simple detection tricky.
> 
> Let me verify by checking if the file size matches the binary STL structure: if it's truly binary with 225706 triangles, the total should be 84 bytes (header + count) plus 50 bytes per triangle. The file is 11,285,384 bytes, and doing the math: (11,285,384 - 84) / 50 = exactly 225,706 triangles. That's a perfect match, so this is definitely a binary STL file with that many triangles — the "solid Shape0" header is just a naming convention used by slicers like Cura and Slic3r. For a model like 3DBenchy with mostly curved surfaces, I'd expect the planar reconstruction to generate many small patches rather than large flat faces.
> 
> Since the normal pipeline takes about 25 minutes and skipping the curved-fitting stages (stage 2) would save roughly 15 minutes, but stage 3 might now have to handle far more faces than usual (since planar-only reconstruction won't consolidate curved regions into single BSPLINE faces), the overall runtime is unpredictable — it could be faster or slower. I should run this with an increased timeout in the background and start with a smaller test run to validate the approach.
> 
> Let me first do a bounded test that stops after stage 2.6 (face selection) to see how many faces get selected and how long stage 2 takes, which avoids waiting for the full stage 3 and 4 reconstruction that could take much longer with a huge face count. I'm constructing a command that runs the tool with the skip-stage flags to disable the curved-fitting stages and stops at face selection.
> 
> Actually, I should reconsider whether to use `--release` mode given the 225k triangles in the model—debug builds could be prohibitively slow. Let me check the project documentation to see how they typically invoke brepper for benchy runs.

That's because the selection also includes all the single-face fallback hypotheses that don't merge with anything, since there's no absorb or refine happening for single-face-only regions unless they're adjacent to a bigger surface. So 149096 single-face plus 20795 multi-face equals 169891 total hypotheses from 2.1, but stage 2.6 only selects 41324 "surfaces" — that doesn't match. Let me re-read the `select_surfaces` logic to understand what's happening. It looks like Step 2 builds candidates only from hypotheses with more than one face... idates are actually non-overlapping since each face belongs to exactly one planar hypothesis from the BFS algorithm, so the greedy selection would just pick all 20795 multi-face candidates without any real competition, then assign the remaining 149096 faces as single-face hypotheses, totaling 169891 selected surfaces.
> 
> But the actual output was only 41324 — way off from that estimate. I think I'm missing something about how the algorithm works. Let me reconsider: maybe the planar hypothesis count itself changed earlier in the pipeline, or perhaps the `absorb_redundant_surfaces` pass that runs after selection is merging a lot of the single-face and small multi-face selections into larger adjacent ones, which would explain the dramatic reduction. surfaces — so on 3DBenchy's curved hull, the default 0.4mm surface tolerance was loose enough that countless small single-triangle facets got absorbed into nearby larger planar hypotheses, since their chordal deviation stayed within that threshold even though they weren't truly coplanar.
> 
> But I'm realizing this might not actually be the right behavior for what we're trying to achieve. The goal is to represent every non-redundant STL triangle as part of a planar FACE, but that doesn't mean we should group many near-coplanar triangles into one big "planar" face using surface tolerance as acceptable slop — doing so would create a mathematically invalid representation where the face doesn't actually pass through all the absorbed triangle vertices, only comes within 0.4mm of them, which could introduce measurable geometric errors.
> 
> Actually, let me reconsider: the absorption doesn't refit the plane itself, it just reassigns which triangles belong to which face. So the resulting STEP face remains geometrically exact (a true plane), but its boundary vertices might be up to 0.4mm off that plane — it's lossy and approximate, but this is how the existing behavior works.
> 
> The absorption pass is designed to eliminate tangent-junction bridge facets and spurious sub-fits, which is standard throughout the pipeline. For the user's specific request to verify that every non-redundant STL triangle has a planar face, I think the key point is whether stage 2.6 covers all faces with planar hypotheses — which it does, at 100% coverage. Whether those hypotheses later get merged into fewer larger planar STEP faces doesn't violate the requirement.
> 
> That said, since we're skipping curved surface types entirely, the genuinely curved regions like the hull and chimney cylinder will now be approximated by near-planar patches with chordal deviation up to 0.4mm rather than exact analytic surfaces — but that's the intended trade-off here.

<!-- msg_011Cd6e9q3yHaFTaPkivTvZr -->

> I'm tracing through the code to find where the trimmed curve is first constructed—it looks like line 1577 calls the trimmed curve constructor with parameters computed from an earlier block, and I need to carefully follow which variables are being bound to understand the flow.
> 
> The key insight is that `t_start` and `t_end` get rebound by a large if/else block starting around line 1499. In the "trim to vertex endpoints" branch, it projects both vertices to get parameter values, then applies additional logic for closed or periodic curves—but for a normal straight-line edge with two distinct vertices, it just ensures the parameters are ordered correctly so `t_start <= t_end`.
> 
> The bug appears when both vertices project to the same parameter value.  This can happen if the vertices are nearly coincident (within tolerance but still treated as distinct endpoints) or if they happen to project to the same point on the curve. When `t_start == t_end`, the `TrimmedCurve` constructor fails because it requires distinct parameter bounds. Given that the planar-only run generates many tiny, degenerate boundary edges from single-triangle fallback hypotheses, it's very likely some vertex pairs are nearly coincident yet treated as separate endpoints, causing their projections to be equal or extremely close on the underlying curve.
> 
> I should check if there's already an established guard or helper function elsewhere in the codebase (around lines 2933–2938 and 7473) that handles this "zero-length trim" case, so I can reuse it instead of adding ad hoc logic here.

> That's 0.5 nanometers, which is far larger than the 4.25e-14 millimeter separation between these two vertices. They should definitely have been welded if the algorithm actually compared them. The issue is likely that the welding uses spatial hashing or grid bucketing, which can fail to catch coincident points if they hash to different buckets due to floating-point rounding differences. 752	    fn repair_coincident_faces(&mut self, stats: &mut MeshRepairStats) {
753	        // Group triangles over the same vertex set, split by winding: two
754	        // triangles on vertices {a<b<c} have equal winding iff the vertex
755	        // following a in cyclic order is the same.
756	        let mut groups: HashMap<[usize; 3], (Vec<usize>, Vec<usize>)> = HashMap::new();
757	        for (face_idx, face) in self.faces.iter().enumerate() {

760	            }

764	            let pos_min = tri.iter().position(|&v| v == key[0]).unwrap();
765	            let group = groups.entry(key).or_default();

767	                group.0.push(face_idx);

771	        }

773	        let mut alive = vec![true; self.faces.len()];
774	        let mut dropped = 0_usize;

778	            }
779	            let pairs = fwd.len().min(rev.len());
780	            for &f in fwd.iter().take(pairs).chain(rev.iter().take(pairs)) {
781	                alive[f] = false;
782	                dropped += 1;
783	            }
784	            let survivors = if fwd.len() > pairs { &fwd[pairs..] } else { &rev[pairs..] };

802	    /// negative, unless they are voids contained (by bounding box) in a
803	    /// positive closed shell. Components with contradictory orientation
804	    /// (non-orientable) are left untouched for validation to report.

fn refine_corner_vertices(
    vertices: &mut [BRepVertex],
    edges: &[ReconEdge],
    face_descriptors: &[FaceDescriptor],
    stage2: &Stage2Output,
    config: &Config,
) {
    // Two adjacent corners must never refine onto the same point: the edge
    // between them collapses to zero length and MakeEdge rejects it
    // (DifferentPointsOnClosedCurve; even its straight-line fallback needs
    // distinct points). Adjacent surface sets that pin the same intersection
    // point arise when fits are coarser than the mesh — observed on 3DBenchy
    // with --skip-stage forcing planar-only fits, where near-coplanar facet
    // planes gave both endpoints of edge 5715 the same projection fixpoint
    // (4e-14 mm apart). Below this separation from any edge-adjacent corner,
    // the candidate is discarded and the corner keeps the raw mesh position
    // (mesh vertices themselves are always ≥ the weld tolerance apart).
    let min_sep = config.vertex_tolerance_mm.max(1e-6);

    for vi in 0..vertices.len() {
        let vert = &vertices[vi];
        if vert.adjacent_faces.len() < 2 {
            continue;
        }
