> I need to work through the borrow checker issue in the loop — `vert` is an immutable reference to `vertices[vi]` that I'm holding while reading `vert.adjacent_faces`, but then I'm also accessing `vertices[vi].adjacent_edges` and `vertices[ui].point` later, which should be fine since those are all immutable borrows.
> 
> The real constraint is the final assignment `vertices[vi].point = p;` which needs a mutable borrow, so `vert` has to be dead by then. Looking at where `vert` is actually used — the projection loop, near_tangent, and near_bspline checks — those all happen before the mutable assignment, so the borrow should naturally drop and let me proceed.
> 
> One thing I'm noticing though: the original code used `edges.get(ei).is_some_and(...)` for the near_tangent check, which suggests defensive programming against out-of-bounds indices in `adjacent_edges`. I switched to direct indexing, but to match the local style and avoid panics if indices are stale, I should probably use the same `.get()` pattern.

> The root cause is that corner refinement moved each vertex onto its adjacent faces' intersection, and those face pairs are nearly coplanar. The refined positions shifted about 0.02mm in x, which is within the 2× surface tolerance allowance, but that movement happened perpendicular to a shallow dihedral angle. So the perpendicular edge is a downstream consequence of allowing refinement to move vertices that much in directions orthogonal to shallow face pairs. Preventing it would mean tightening the refinement movement allowance, but that conflicts with the designed contract that vertices can move up to 2× surface tolerance.
> 
> The real fix belongs in build_pcurve — that's where the code assumed UV samples would always be distinct, but degenerate edges (where a point maps to a single UV location in one face) are valid in-tolerance geometry that OCCT explicitly supports. The pcurve builder needs to handle whatever in-tolerance geometry exists, including edges that collapse to a point in one face's UV space. And yes, the near-parallel tangent plane classification is correct even in planar-only mode — adjacent facets with dihedral angle less than 25° are tangent by design, which is what angular_tolerance controls.
> 
> For the fully-degenerate case with a 2-point degree-1 B-spline, I need to mark the edge properly in OCCT: an edge with a 3D curve plus a pointlike pcurve on one face and a normal pcurve on the other, with edge tolerance set high enough to cover the maximum deviation. This isn't a "degenerate edge" in OCCT's sense (those are for edges with no 3D curve poles) — it's a regular edge with a constrained pcurve. BRepCheck's SameParameter validation will verify the 3D curve matches the pcurves through their surfaces, and the deviation should stay within the edge tolerance we set.
> 
> Now I need to check what 2D B-spline constructors are available in the codebase — looking at the geom2d imports in stage3.rs to see if there's a BSplineCurve binding or if I need to use the geom2d_api layer.

```
/// 0..n-3 with end multiplicity 4.
fn clamped_cubic_knots(n: usize) -> (OwnedPtr<t_col_std::Array1OfReal>, OwnedPtr<t_col_std::Array1OfInteger>) {
    let nk = n - 2; // distinct knots for degree 3
    let mut knots = t_col_std::Array1OfReal::new_int2(1, nk as i32);
    let mut mults = t_col_std::Array1OfInteger::new_int2(1, nk as i32);
    for k in 0..nk {
        knots.set_value(k as i32 + 1, &(k as f64));
        let m = if k == 0 || k == nk - 1 { 4 } else { 1 };
        mults.set_value(k as i32 + 1, &m);
    }
    (knots, mults)
}

/// Exact bicubic B-spline surface from an n×n pole lattice on a regular XY
/// grid (spacing \`d\`, lower corner \`x0\`,\`y0\`), pole heights from \`zf\`.
/// Poles on a regular lattice make x depend only on u and y only on v.
```

```
        // Geom2dAPI_Interpolate throws Standard_ConstructionError on
        // consecutive points closer than its tolerance, and UV samples are
        // not guaranteed distinct: an edge (nearly) perpendicular to this
        // face's fitted surface projects to (nearly) one UV point — observed
        // on 3DBenchy planar-only fits, where corner refinement legitimately
        // offsets both endpoints of a tangent plane-plane edge along the
        // face normal within the fit slop. Interpolate through the samples
        // that are pairwise-distinct in UV; if the whole projection is
        // pointlike, use a degree-1 pcurve (possibly constant) between the
        // endpoint samples instead — the edge's 3D extent then shows up in
        // the returned deviation, which the caller folds into the edge
        // tolerance like any other pcurve deviation.
        const UV_DISTINCT_EPS: f64 = 1e-8; // 100× the interpolation tolerance
        let eps2 = UV_DISTINCT_EPS * UV_DISTINCT_EPS;
        let d2 = |i: usize, j: usize| -> f64 {
            (uv.us[i] - uv.us[j]).powi(2) + (uv.vs[i] - uv.vs[j]).powi(2)
        };
        let last = uv.ts.len() - 1;
        let mut kept: Vec<usize> = vec![0];
        for i in 1..last {
            if d2(i, *kept.last().unwrap()) >= eps2 {
                kept.push(i);
            }
        }
        // The final (possibly vertex-snapped) sample must be kept exactly:
        // drop earlier samples that crowd it.
        while kept.len() > 1 && d2(last, *kept.last().unwrap()) < eps2 {
            kept.pop();
        }
        let pointlike = kept.len() == 1 && d2(last, 0) < eps2;
        if !pointlike {
            kept.push(last);
        }

        let pcurve = if pointlike {
            let mut poles = t_colgp::Array1OfPnt2d::new_int2(1, 2);
            poles.set_value(1, &gp::Pnt2d::new_real2(uv.us[0], uv.vs[0]));
            poles.set_value(2, &gp::Pnt2d::new_real2(uv.us[last], uv.vs[last]));
            let mut knots = t_col_std::Array1OfReal::new_int2(1, 2);
            knots.set_value(1, &uv.ts[0]);
            knots.set_value(2, &uv.ts[last]);
            let mut mults = t_col_std::Array1OfInteger::new_int2(1, 2);
            mults.set_value(1, &2);
            mults.set_value(2, &2);
            let bs = geom2d::BSplineCurve::new_array1ofpnt2d_array1ofreal_array1ofinteger_int_bool(
                &poles, &knots, &mults, 1, false,
            );
            geom2d::BSplineCurve::to_handle(bs).to_handle_curve()
        } else {
            let np = kept.len() as i32;
            let mut pts_h = t_colgp::HArray1OfPnt2d::new_int2(1, np);
            let mut params_h = t_col_std::HArray1OfReal::new_int2(1, np);
            for (slot, &i) in kept.iter().enumerate() {
                let pnt2d = gp::Pnt2d::new_real2(uv.us[i], uv.vs[i]);
                pts_h.change_array1().set_value((slot + 1) as i32, &pnt2d);
                params_h.change_array1().set_value((slot + 1) as i32, &uv.ts[i]);
            }
            let pts_handle = t_colgp::HArray1OfPnt2d::to_handle(pts_h);
            let params_handle = t_col_std::HArray1OfReal::to_handle(params_h);
            let mut interp = geom2d_api::Interpolate::new_handletcolgpharray1ofpnt2d_handletcolstdharray1ofreal_bool_real(
                &pts_handle, &params_handle, false, 1e-10,
            );
            interp.perform();
            if !interp.is_done() {
                return Err(Stage3Error::AdjacencyError(
                    "pcurve interpolation failed".into(),
                ));
            }
            interp.curve().to_handle_curve()
        };
```
</details>

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011Cd6hNh9g5ctVeHjDgp1N9 -->

Done — **3DBenchy planar-only now runs end-to-end**: 704MB STEP file, one closed solid, volume 15393.7mm³ (matching the mesh volume, as a faceted model should), with every one of the 196,393 non-redundant mesh faces covered by one of 41,324 planar faces.

The "vertex-welding issue" turned out not to be a welding issue at all. Thinking it through before patching paid off: the load-time weld is grid-based with 27-neighbor checks, so it *guarantees* no two distinct mesh vertices are within tolerance, and stage-1 repair never creates vertices — meaning a 4.25e-14mm pair was impossible in the mesh. The coincident points were **BRepVertex** positions, and `BRepVertex.point` has exactly two writers: initialization from the mesh, and stage-3 corner refinement. That narrowed it to one culprit, confirmed empirically on the first run.

Two fixes landed (plus the trim-span guard from earlier, which was the same root cause seen upstream):

1. **Corner-refinement collapse guard** ([stage3.rs:712](src/stage3.rs:712)): cyclic projection was pulling *both* endpoints of edge 5715 onto the same near-parallel-plane intersection fixpoint. A refined position landing within `max(vertex_tolerance, 1e-6mm)` of an edge-adjacent corner is now rejected — the vertex keeps its raw mesh position, same conservative fallback as the existing divergence guard. It fired exactly once on benchy, for exactly the failing vertex. I chose this over merging the collapsed vertices because merging needs topology surgery across edges/faces/wires for a case that only means "the fits couldn't resolve a feature the mesh did" — keeping mesh positions is the honest answer.

2. **Pointlike pcurve projections** ([stage3.rs:5454](src/stage3.rs:5454)): the next crash behind it was `Geom2dAPI_Interpolate` throwing on an edge that runs *perpendicular* to the face it bounds (edge 25813 vs face 7145 — legal in-tolerance sloppiness, 0.02mm ≪ 0.4mm surface tolerance), so all its UV samples coincided. `build_pcurve` now dedupes UV-coincident samples, and a fully pointlike projection gets a degree-1 two-pole B-spline pcurve (coincident poles are legal, parameterization exact), with the edge's 3D extent folded into the edge tolerance — which is OCCT's intended tolerance model for exactly this.

Verification: full 494-test suite green after each fix, clippy clean, and the benchy log shows both fixes doing their jobs. One honest caveat: the resulting solid draws a `BRepCheck validation` **warning** (not a failure) — unsurprising for 41k tolerance-stretched facet planes, noted in [TODO.md](TODO.md) as a quality follow-up if planar-only output ever needs to be more than a robustness probe. All three fixes harden shared stage-3 paths, so they may also help the normal curved pipeline's uncharted stage 3.4+ territory.

Everything is in the working tree, uncommitted — say the word if you'd like a commit.

```
Fixes the TODO crash (Geom_TrimmedCurve::parameters out of range at edge
2024) and five failures behind it, each diagnosed from a full-model run:

1. candidate_trim_parameters (new): the chain repair's trim_to_endpoints
   ran modulo-2pi arc selection on the bounded, non-periodic B-spline
   candidate, producing trim parameters ~2pi outside its domain. Periodic
   candidates keep the chain-supported arc selection (the TrimmedCurve
   ctor unwraps to and range-checks against the *basis* curve, so wrap
   past 2pi is fine on trimmed circles); bounded candidates get ordered,
   domain-clamped parameters; degenerate spans are rejected.
2. reconstruct_full_circle: sampling a degenerate IntSS line at its
   infinite parameter bounds (~1e100) overflowed the cross product's
   squared norm to inf, slipping past the n2 < 1e-30 collinearity guard;
   gp_Dir then panicked on n/inf = 0. Added finiteness guards.
3. GeomAPI_IntSS construction can throw ("BSpline curve: Knots interval
   values too close" on a bspline-bspline junction) instead of reporting
   failure; wrapped in catch_unwind and routed to the chain fallback.
4. Micro-edges whose trimmed curve endpoints coincide within
   Precision::Confusion made MakeEdge fail with
   DifferentPointsOnClosedCurve; fall back to the straight vertex-to-
   vertex edge, as for curve-less edges.
5. Closed-loop edges had no last-resort curve (the chain fallback
   demanded vertex endpoints; tangent failures never tried it).
   Extracted closed_chain_periodic_bspline, used it for closed-loop
   edges in edge_curve_from_boundary_chain, and wired the chain fallback
   into the tangent-edge failure path (132 failed edge curves -> 1).
6. 3-vertex closed loops are below periodic interpolation's minimum;
   the circle through the three chain points is exact (1 -> 0 failed).

Unit tests pin the trim-parameter and overflow regressions (each
demonstrated to panic against the pre-fix code) and the seam-crossing
arc selection. 3DBenchy now reaches stage 3.4, where it aborts on a face
whose hypothesis spans three disconnected mesh regions - recorded in
TODO.md as the new active item with evidence and a suggested fix
(connected-component splitting of face hypotheses upstream of the seam
planner, which is topologically the wrong place to relax).
```

```
selected planar surfaces, 100% coverage, stage 2 in ~3s vs ~15min)
surfaced three stage-3 defects on shared code paths, each root-caused
and fixed:

1. compute_edge_curve: Geom_TrimmedCurve construction had no degenerate-
   span guard, so both edge endpoints projecting to the same curve
   parameter threw Standard_ConstructionError "U1 == U2". Now returns
   Err below a 1e-9 span (routing to the mesh-boundary-chain fallback),
   matching the existing guard in candidate_trim_parameters.

   identical points. A refined position landing within

   position is now rejected; the corner keeps the raw mesh position.
   (Not a mesh-weld gap: the weld guarantees distinct mesh vertices are
   at least the weld tolerance apart and repair creates no vertices —
   only refinement can mint sub-tolerance pairs.)

3. build_pcurve: UV interpolation samples are not guaranteed distinct —
   an edge (nearly) perpendicular to a face's fitted plane (both
   endpoints legitimately offset along the normal by corner refinement,
   within its 2x surface-tolerance contract) projects to a single UV

   edge range), with the edge's 3D extent folded into the returned
   deviation and hence the edge tolerance.
```

```
 *Comment on 2.1: The DFS vs BFS question is worth considering: DFS can get "trapped" in a narrow corridor and accumulate drift before exploring the main region. BFS explores more uniformly. However, the re-fitting step partially mitigates this. A bigger issue: once you re-fit the plane, previously accepted faces might no longer fit the new plane! Consider a final validation pass that removes faces whose vertices exceed the tolerance after the final fit. Also: the "vertices in right-hand-rule order" for the hypothesis is unclear—planar regions aren't simply connected in general (they can have holes), so you'll need a more complex boundary representation.*
 
-### 2.2 Deduce cylindrical hypotheses
+#### 2.2 Deduce cylindrical hypotheses
 TODO. Optional: Worthwhile to generalize to conic/cylindrical? Be sure to handle surfaces with negative curavature correctly - negative radius?
 
 *Comment: Cylinders are parameterized by axis (point + direction) and radius—6 DOF total. Minimum 5 points needed for a unique fit, but robust fitting requires more. Key considerations: (1) A cylindrical patch has principal curvature in one direction only—use this to distinguish from spheres/cones. (2) "Negative radius" isn't the right framing; instead, track whether the surface normal points toward or away from the axis (convex vs concave). (3) For cones: 7 DOF (axis point, direction, half-angle). Cones degenerate to cylinders when half-angle→0, so you might fit cones first and detect near-zero angles. (4) Watch out for nearly-planar cylindrical patches (large radius)—they may fit planes better.*
```

```
author: Seth LaForge <sethml@ofb.net>
date: Sun Mar 1 19:41:46 2026 -0800

Update instructions.

===

See [DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md](DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md) for full documentation.

### Tolerance Settings

#### Surface Tolerance

This is how far the surface of a mesh triangle is allowed to deviate from the surface it is approximating.

Onshape calls this "Chordal tolerance (mm)", and the preset values are:
- Coarse: 0.24 mm
- Medium: 0.12 mm
- Fine: 0.06 mm

Fusion 360 calls this "Surface Deviation", and the preset values seem to vary based on model size:
- Low: 1x
- Medium: 2.5x
- High: 8x

#### Angular Tolerance

This is the maximum angle between adjacent triangle faces that approximate a smooth surface.

Onshape calls this "Angular deviation (deg)", and the present values are:
- Coarse: 12.5 deg
```

```
3886:      "content": "        # Run tool, capture stderr for diagnostics and stdout for distances\n        diag_file=$(mktemp)\n        output=$($BINARY \"$stl_file\" \"$step_file\" 2>\"$diag_file\") || {\n            printf \"%-40s %15s\\n\" \"$base\" \"ERROR\"\n            rm -f \"$diag_file\"\n            continue\n        }\n\n        # Parse stdout (tab-separated: vtx_max_dist ctr_max_dist)\n        vtx_max_dist=$(echo \"$output\" | cut -f1)\n        ctr_max_dist=$(echo \"$output\" | cut -f2)\n\n        # Parse diagnostic output\n        nodes=$(grep \"^STL:\" \"$diag_file\" | sed 's/STL: \\([0-9]*\\) nodes.*/\\1/')\n        faces=$(grep \"^STEP:\" \"$diag_file\" | sed 's/STEP: \\([0-9]*\\) faces/\\1/')\n        vtx_avg_dist=$(grep \"^Vertex\" \"$diag_file\" | sed 's/.*avg: \\([^ ]*\\).*/\\1/')\n        ctr_avg_dist=$(grep \"^Centroid\" \"$diag_file\" | sed 's/.*avg: \\([^ ]*\\).*/\\1/')\n        rm -f \"$diag_file\"\n\n        printf \"%-40s %15s %15s %15s %15s %8s %8s\\n\" \"$base\" \"$vtx_max_dist\" \"$vtx_avg_dist\" \"$ctr_max_dist\" \"$ctr_avg_dist\" \"$nodes\" \"$faces\"",

4521:      "content": "/// Compute maximum distance between STL mesh vertices/centroids and STEP model surfaces.\n///\n/// Reads an STL file to extract vertex positions and triangle centroids, reads a\n/// STEP file to extract surfaces, and computes the minimum distance from each\n/// point to any STEP surface. Reports max distance for both vertices and centroids.\n///\n/// Units: distances are in the same units as the STEP file (typically mm).\nuse opencascade_sys::{\n    b_rep, extrema, geom_api, gp, message, rw_stl, step_control, top_abs, top_exp, topo_ds,\n};",
4564:      "content": "#!/bin/bash\n# Run step-distance on all STL/STEP pairs under tests/ and print a table\n# with per-group (suffix) summary rows.\nset -euo pipefail\n\nSCRIPT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$0\")\" && pwd)\"\nPROJECT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$SCRIPT_DIR/..\" && pwd)\"\nBINARY=\"$PROJECT_DIR/target/release/step-distance\"\n\n# Build release binary\necho \"Building step-distance (release)...\" >&2\ncargo build --release --bin step-distance --manifest-path \"$PROJECT_DIR/Cargo.toml\" 2>&1 | tail -1 >&2\n\n# Format: columns are test case, vtx max, vtx avg, ctr max, ctr avg, max dim, nodes, faces\n# All distances in microns (µm). Max dimension in model units.\nHDR_FMT=\"%-40s %12s %12s %12s %12s %10s %8s %8s\\n\"\nROW_FMT=\"%-40s %12s %12s %12s %12s %10s %8s %8s\\n\"\nSEP_FMT=\"%-40s %12s %12s %12s %12s %10s %8s %8s\\n\"\n\nprint_header() {\n    printf \"$HDR_FMT\" \"Test Case\" \"Vtx Max µm\" \"Vtx Avg µm\" \"Ctr Max µm\" \"Ctr Avg µm\" \"Max Dim\" \"Nodes\" \"Faces\"\n    printf \"$SEP_FMT\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..40})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..10})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})\"\n}\n\n# Convert scientific notation value to microns, formatted as e.g. \"0.13e-6\" style\n# Input: value in model units. Output: value * 1e6 in scientific notation.\nto_microns() {\n    awk \"BEGIN { v = $1 * 1e6; if (v == 0) printf \\\"0\\\"; else printf \\\"%.2e\\\", v }\"\n}\n\n# Track max values per group for summary rows\ndeclare -A group_vtx_max group_vtx_avg group_ctr_max group_ctr_avg\n\nupdate_group_max() {\n    local group=\"$1\" vtx_max=\"$2\" vtx_avg=\"$3\" ctr_max=\"$4\" ctr_avg=\"$5\"\n    if [ -z \"${group_vtx_max[$group]:-}\" ]; then\n        group_vtx_max[$group]=\"$vtx_max\"\n        group_vtx_avg[$group]=\"$vtx_avg\"\n        group_ctr_max[$group]=\"$ctr_max\"\n        group_ctr_avg[$group]=\"$ctr_avg\"\n    else\n        group_vtx_max[$group]=$(awk \"BEGIN { a=${group_vtx_max[$group]}; b=$vtx_max; print (a>b) ? a : b }\")\n        group_vtx_avg[$group]=$(awk \"BEGIN { a=${group_vtx_avg[$group]}; b=$vtx_avg; print (a>b) ? a : b }\")\n        group_ctr_max[$group]=$(awk \"BEGIN { a=${group_ctr_max[$group]}; b=$ctr_max; print (a>b) ? a : b }\")\n        group_ctr_avg[$group]=$(awk \"BEGIN { a=${group_ctr_avg[$group]}; b=$ctr_avg; print (a>b) ? a : b }\")\n    fi\n}\n\nprint_group_summary() {\n    local group=\"$1\"\n    if [ -n \"${group_vtx_max[$group]:-}\" ]; then\n        local vm=$(to_microns \"${group_vtx_max[$group]}\")\n        local va=$(to_microns \"${group_vtx_avg[$group]}\")\n        local cm=$(to_microns \"${group_ctr_max[$group]}\")\n        local ca=$(to_microns \"${group_ctr_avg[$group]}\")\n        printf \"$ROW_FMT\" \"  ** MAX ($group) **\" \"$vm\" \"$va\" \"$cm\" \"$ca\" \"\" \"\"

4699:      "content": "#!/bin/bash\n# Run step-distance on all STL/STEP pairs under tests/ and print a table\n# with per-group (suffix) summary rows.\n# Distances displayed in microns (µm). Max dimension in model units.\nset -euo pipefail\n\nSCRIPT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$0\")\" && pwd)\"\nPROJECT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$SCRIPT_DIR/..\" && pwd)\"\nBINARY=\"$PROJECT_DIR/target/release/step-distance\"\n\n# Build release binary\necho \"Building step-distance (release)...\" >&2\ncargo build --release --bin step-distance --manifest-path \"$PROJECT_DIR/Cargo.toml\" 2>&1 | tail -1 >&2\n\nROW_FMT=\"%-40s %12s %12s %12s %12s %10s %8s %8s\\n\"\n\nprint_header() {\n    printf \"$ROW_FMT\" \"Test Case\" \"Vtx Max µm\" \"Vtx Avg µm\" \"Ctr Max µm\" \"Ctr Avg µm\" \"Max Dim\" \"Nodes\" \"Faces\"\n    print_sep\n}\n\nprint_sep() {\n    printf \"$ROW_FMT\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..40})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..10})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})\" \\\n        \"$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})\"\n}\n\n# Convert value to microns for display\nto_microns() {\n    awk \"BEGIN { v = $1 * 1e6; if (v == 0) printf \\\"0\\\"; else printf \\\"%.2e\\\", v }\"\n}\n\n# Collect all STL files into an array (avoids subshell from pipe)\nmapfile -t stl_files < <(find \"$PROJECT_DIR/tests\" -name \"*.stl\" -not -name \"*.ascii.stl\" | sort)\n\n# Track per-group maximums\ndeclare -A group_vtx_max group_vtx_avg group_ctr_max group_ctr_avg\nprev_dir=\"\"\ngroup_order=()\n\nflush_groups() {\n    if [ ${#group_order[@]} -gt 0 ]; then\n        for g in \"${group_order[@]}\"; do\n            local vm=$(to_microns \"${group_vtx_max[$g]}\")\n            local va=$(to_microns \"${group_vtx_avg[$g]}\")\n            local cm=$(to_microns \"${group_ctr_max[$g]}\")\n            local ca=$(to_microns \"${group_ctr_avg[$g]}\")\n            printf \"$ROW_FMT\" \"  ** MAX ($g) **\" \"$vm\" \"$va\" \"$cm\" \"$ca\" \"\" \"\" \"\"\n        done\n    fi\n    group_vtx_max=(); group_vtx_avg=(); group_ctr_max=(); group_ctr_avg=()\n    group_order=()\n}\n\nupdate_group_max() {\n    local group=\"$1\" vm=\"$2\" va=\"$3\" cm=\"$4\" ca=\"$5\"\n    if [ -z \"${group_vtx_max[$group]:-}\" ]; then\n        group_vtx_max[$group]=\"$vm\"\n        group_vtx_avg[$group]=\"$va\"\n        group_ctr_max[$group]=\"$cm\"\n        group_ctr_avg[$group]=\"$ca\"\n        group_order+=(\"$group\")\n    else\n        group_vtx_max[$group]=$(awk \"BEGIN { a=${group_vtx_max[$group]}; b=$vm; print (a>b) ? a : b }\")\n        group_vtx_avg[$group]=$(awk \"BEGIN { a=${group_vtx_avg[$group]}; b=$va; print (a>b) ? a : b }\")\n        group_ctr_max[$group]=$(awk \"BEGIN { a=${group_ctr_max[$group]}; b=$cm; print (a>b) ? a : b }\")\n        group_ctr_avg[$group]=$(awk \"BEGIN { a=${group_ctr_avg[$group]}; b=$ca; print (a>b) ? a : b }\")\n
```

## Was `--surface-tolerance=0.4` ever increased?

**No — it has been 0.4 since the very first line of brepper CLI code**, and it never changed. The only tolerance default ever bumped in code was the *vertex* tolerance (1e-5 → 5e-5, commit `75870db`, 2026-03-20, "bump default vertex tolerance").

## Where the 0.4 came from

The trail leads to **your own hand edit**, all on 2026-03-01:

- **10:38–13:25** — You built centroid-distance tracking into `step-distance` and measured every STL/STEP pair. (Caveat when reading those logs: until the 13:25 fix, the µm column was inflated 1000× — "2.60e+04 µm" really meant 0.026mm.) Real measured worst-case chordal sag: **0.026mm** (Onshape coarse plate). The same morning you documented the tessellator presets in the README: Onshape chordal tolerance Coarse = 0.24mm, Medium = 0.12mm, Fine = 0.06mm.
- **19:41, commit `3a62481` "Update instructions."** — a no-agent, hand-written commit that rewrote the CLI spec from the earlier meters-based draft (`--surface-tolerance=<meters>`, **no default at all**; vertex "1e-6 = 1 micron") to STL units with "**default 0.4 mm**", and added the Fusion `plate_with_hole` low/medium/high models.
- **19:44, commit `28dfbe9`** — the program-skeleton agent session implemented the config exactly per that README, and 0.4 has ridden along unchanged through the clap rewrite and everything since.

No rationale was recorded anywhere — commit message is just "Update instructions." But the same-day context makes it legible: **0.4 ≈ Onshape's Coarse preset promise (0.24mm) with ~1.7× headroom** — an *acceptance* bound meant to never falsely reject any tessellation a mainstream CAD tool emits, even at its coarsest setting. It was never sized to observed data: I re-measured the Fusion plates today and the worst real deviation in the corpus is 0.038mm (Fusion-low), so the default sits 10–15× above actual tessellation sag.

If it feels too high, that's because it's doing rejection-bound duty, not precision duty — and that slack propagates: it's the centroid check in BFS growth, the absorb-pass merge tolerance, the 2× movement cap in corner refinement, and the `--compare` gate. There's already a related deferred note in TODO-adjacent memory: the §2.7 planarize gate sized off `vertex_tolerance` is arguably 25–50× too tight while `surface_tolerance` is arguably loose — if you want, a follow-up could split the two roles (a measured-per-model chordal bound for acceptance vs. the CLI value as a hard ceiling).

```
// ===========================================================================
// Stage 2.6: Surface selection tests
// ===========================================================================

fn config_for_stl_stage26(stl_path: &str) -> config::Config {
    config::parse_config_from(["brepper", stl_path, "--stage=2.6", "-q"]).unwrap()
}

fn config_for_compare_stage26(stl_path: &str, step_path: &str) -> config::Config {
    let mut config =
        config::parse_config_from(["brepper", stl_path, "--compare", step_path, "--stage=2.6", "-q"])
            .unwrap();
    config.load_compare_step().unwrap();
    config
}

/// Generate a test that runs stage 2.6 with --compare against a STEP file.
macro_rules! test_stl_step_stage26_compare {
    ($name:ident, $stl_path:literal, $step_path:literal) => {
        #[test]
        fn $name() {
            let stl = format!("{}/{}", manifest_dir(), $stl_path);
            let step = format!("{}/{}", manifest_dir(), $step_path);
            let config = config_for_compare_stage26(&stl, &step);
            run_stage2(&config);
        }
    };
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Surface selection count tests
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[test]
fn cube_surface_selection_all_planar() {
    let stl = format!("{}/tests/ccad/generated/cube.stl", manifest_dir());
    let config = config_for_stl_stage26(&stl);
    let output = run_stage2(&config);

    assert!(!output.selected_surfaces.is_empty(), "should have selected surfaces");
    // Cube: 6 faces, all single-face planar hypotheses, no other hypotheses
    let planar_count = output.selected_surfaces.iter()
        .filter(|s| matches!(s, stage2::SelectedSurface::Planar(_)))
        .count();
    assert_eq!(planar_count, output.selected_surfaces.len(), "cube should select only planar surfaces");
}

#[test]
fn block_with_hole_surface_selection() {
    let stl = format!("{}/tests/ccad/generated/block_with_hole.stl", manifest_dir());
    let config = config_for_stl_stage26(&stl);
    let output = run_stage2(&config);

    // block_with_hole: 6 planar faces + 1 cylindrical hole = 7 selected surfaces
    let planar_count = output.selected_surfaces.iter()
        .filter(|s| matches!(s, stage2::SelectedSurface::Planar(_)))
        .count();
    let cyl_count = output.selected_surfaces.iter()
        .filter(|s| is_selected_cylinder(s, &output))
        .count();
    assert_eq!(planar_count, 6, "block_with_hole should have 6 planar surfaces");
    assert_eq!(cyl_count, 1, "block_with_hole should have 1 cylindrical surface");
    assert_eq!(output.selected_surfaces.len(), 7, "block_with_hole should have 7 total selected surfaces");
}

#[test]
fn pipe_surface_selection() {
    let stl = format!("{}/tests/ccad/generated/pipe.stl", manifest_dir());

        .count();

    assert_eq!(cyl_count, 2, "pipe should have 2 cylindrical surfaces");
}
```

```
    // Merge spurious sub-fit surfaces and tangent-junction orphan facets
    // into larger adjacent surfaces (including freshly fitted B-splines)
    // before reconstruction.
    absorb_redundant_surfaces(
        &mesh, &mut selected_surfaces, &mut planar_hypotheses,
        &mut conical_hypotheses, &mut spherical_hypotheses,
        &mut toroidal_hypotheses, &mut bspline_hypotheses, config,
    );

    // Move border faces to the adjacent surface that fits them better —
    // misassigned rows shift reconstructed edge/corner positions off the
    // true feature curves (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN §2.6 surface refitting).
    refine_boundary_assignments(
        &mesh, &mut selected_surfaces, &mut planar_hypotheses,
        &mut conical_hypotheses, &mut spherical_hypotheses,
        &mut toroidal_hypotheses, &mut bspline_hypotheses, config,
    );
```

**After:**
```
    // Stage 2.8: Merge spurious sub-fit surfaces and tangent-junction orphan
    // facets into larger adjacent surfaces (including freshly fitted
    // B-splines) before reconstruction. The loose (≤2-face orphan) rule
    // accepts up to the chordal surface tolerance of slop, so absorbed
    // facets no longer lie exactly on their assigned surface —
    // --skip-stage=2.8 disables this when exact per-facet geometry matters
    // (e.g. planar-only mode).
    if config.stage.at_least(2, 8) && !config.skip_stage(2, 8) {
        let t = Instant::now();
        let merged = absorb_redundant_surfaces(
            &mesh, &mut selected_surfaces, &mut planar_hypotheses,
            &mut conical_hypotheses, &mut spherical_hypotheses,
            &mut toroidal_hypotheses, &mut bspline_hypotheses, config,
        );
        if config.verbosity >= 1 {
            eprintln!(
                "Stage 2.8 ({:.3}s): Absorbed {} redundant surface(s) ({} remaining)",
                t.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
                merged,
                selected_surfaces.len(),
            );
        }
    } else if config.skip_stage(2, 8) && config.verbosity >= 1 {
        eprintln!("Stage 2.8: skipped (--skip-stage)");
    }

    // Stage 2.9: Move border faces to the adjacent surface that fits them
    // better — misassigned rows shift reconstructed edge/corner positions
    // off the true feature curves (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN §2.6 surface refitting).
    if config.stage.at_least(2, 9) && !config.skip_stage(2, 9) {
        let t = Instant::now();
        let moved = refine_boundary_assignments(
            &mesh, &mut selected_surfaces, &mut planar_hypotheses,
            &mut conical_hypotheses, &mut spherical_hypotheses,
            &mut toroidal_hypotheses, &mut bspline_hypotheses, config,
        );
        if config.verbosity >= 1 {
            eprintln!(
                "Stage 2.9 ({:.3}s): Boundary refinement reassigned {} face(s)",
                t.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
                moved,
            );
        }
    } else if config.skip_stage(2, 9) && config.verbosity >= 1 {
        eprintln!("Stage 2.9: skipped (--skip-stage)");
    }
```

```
222:Effect: interior seeds run first and commit large, correctly-fitted hypotheses; junction faces are usually absorbed by BFS growth before they ever become start faces (committed faces do not seed). **Seed gathering exclusion**: in pass 1, junction faces are also excluded from seed neighborhoods (conical fans, spherical 1-rings, toroidal hop-expansion), so seeds stay on one surface; a second pass retries remaining undeduced faces without the filter so surfaces composed entirely of transition faces (one-row fillets) still seed. Toroidal gathering hop-expands (up to 3 hops, targeting ≥ 16 faces) since small fillet tubes have little interior across the tube but extend along the fillet axis. Ordering and masks are deterministic: ties break by face index.
267:*expansion* may still absorb them, so tangent junctions work.
380:6. **Absorb redundant surfaces** (\`absorb_redundant_surfaces\`): merge a selected surface S into an adjacent, larger-area surface T when all of S's vertices lie within tolerance of T's fitted geometry AND all of S's face centroids lie within \`--surface-tolerance\` of T. Two situations require this:
382:   - *Tangent-junction bridge facets*: a mesh facet spanning a tangent junction (e.g. cone–sphere on the nosecone) has vertices on both sides and can't be absorbed by either surface at vertex tolerance; it becomes an orphan single-face planar surface creating fake edges. Tolerance: \`--surface-tolerance\` (chordal sag), only for surfaces with ≤ 2 faces.
383:   The centroid check prevents absorbing flat end caps triangulated as a fan from a boundary vertex (all cap *vertices* lie on the adjacent cone/cylinder's boundary circle, but face interiors do not).
401:After selection and absorption, some border faces may have been absorbed by a hypothesis that fits within tolerance but isn't the best fit; the misassignment shifts the mesh region boundary by a row, which shifts reconstructed edge boundary vertices and corner vertices off the true feature curves.
413:For groups of adjacent faces that remain as single-face planar selections after selection (i.e., they fit no analytic surface), fit a B-spline surface. Runs on the raw selection, **before** \`absorb_redundant_surfaces\`: the absorb pass legitimately grows small accidental analytic fits (cylinder ribbons on a freeform surface) with the exactly-on-surface facets around them, which would hide them from region gathering. Absorption and boundary refinement run afterwards, with B-spline surfaces excluded as targets (the fit only vouches for the faces it was built from; a chordal absorb would extend the face into the surface's extrapolation zone).
416:1. **Gather** connected components of single-face planar selections over SMOOTH mesh edges (face-normal step ≤ \`--angular-tolerance\` — a freeform surface varies by at most the tessellation's angular step between neighbors; a 45° chamfer junction is a real feature edge and must split components). The candidate set also includes selections of ≤ 16 faces of any type: freeform surfaces bait the analytic detectors locally (accidentally-coplanar strip pairs at wave inflections, 6-face cylinder ribbons carving up the saddle, 9–12-face cones on a Gaussian bump peak), and left selected they punch holes in the region and leave fake feature edges. Two guards protect real small features: a component with no single-face planar member is never fitted, and when absorbed non-single faces would outnumber the single-face leftovers the component is stripped back to the leftovers.
```
</details>

<details>
<summary>Tool Result</summary>

```
   - \`surface_type: SurfaceType\` — enum: Planar, Cylindrical, Spherical, Conical, Toroidal, BSpline.

   - \`hypothesis_index: usize\` — index into the appropriate hypothesis vector in Stage2Output.
   - \`faces: Vec<usize>\` — mesh face indices assigned to this surface.
   - \`vertices: Vec<usize>\` — mesh vertex indices on this surface (union of face vertices).
6. **Absorb redundant surfaces** (\`absorb_redundant_surfaces\`): merge a selected surface S into an adjacent, larger-area surface T when all of S's vertices lie within tolerance of T's fitted geometry AND all of S's face centroids lie within \`--surface-tolerance\` of T. Two situations require this:
   - *Spurious sub-fits*: a narrow band of faces on a cone/sphere can fit an "exact" cylinder (e.g. 3 azimuthal strips of a shallow cone have nearly coplanar normals) and get committed before the real surface's BFS reaches those faces. Tolerance: 10× vertex tolerance (exact overlap).
   - *Tangent-junction bridge facets*: a mesh facet spanning a tangent junction (e.g. cone–sphere on the nosecone) has vertices on both sides and can't be absorbed by either surface at vertex tolerance; it becomes an orphan single-face planar surface creating fake edges. Tolerance: \`--surface-tolerance\` (chordal sag), only for surfaces with ≤ 2 faces.
   The centroid check prevents absorbing flat end caps triangulated as a fan from a boundary vertex (all cap *vertices* lie on the adjacent cone/cylinder's boundary circle, but face interiors do not).
7. **Prune unselected hypotheses**: remove any hypothesis that was not selected from the hypothesis vectors in \`Stage2Output\`. Remap \`SelectedSurface\` indices to match the compacted vectors. After this step, \`Stage2Output.conical_hypotheses\` (etc.) contain only selected hypotheses.
8. Validate: every face is assigned to exactly one selected surface.
9. Print a summary: count of selected surfaces by type, total faces covered.

**Why this works better than a fixed type-priority rule:** A fixed priority (e.g., spherical > cylindrical > multi-face planar) fails when a bogus hypothesis of a high-priority type (3-face r=139mm sphere) competes with a correct hypothesis of a lower-priority type (35-face r=2mm cylinder). Area-based greedy selection naturally picks the correct hypothesis because it covers vastly more area.

**Nerfed tests:**
- (none currently — \`onshape_pipe_elbow_stage26\` now passes \`--compare\`, see note below.)
- \`onshape_pipe_elbow_stage26_compare\`: Passes \`--compare\` with 290 cylindrical + 0 toroidal selections. The pipe elbow's torus surface is tessellated into many narrow strips whose normals span < 180°, so stage 2.5 torus fitting doesn't fire (normals look locally cylindrical). This is expected — the torus is decomposed into cylindrical patches that individually pass \`--compare\`.

- With the \`--compare\` flag, for each selected surface:
    - Compute face centroids and project them onto the selected surface.
    - Measure distance from each projected centroid to the nearest surface in the reference STEP file.
    - For all hypotheses, report an error if distance exceeds \`--surface-tolerance\`.

#### 2.6 Surface refitting (boundary refinement — implemented)

After selection and absorption, some border faces may have been absorbed by a hypothesis that fits within tolerance but isn't the best fit; the misassignment shifts the mesh region boundary by a row, which shifts reconstructed edge boundary vertices and corner vertices off the true feature curves.

**Algorithm (\`refine_boundary_assignments\`, as implemented):**
1. Build the face → selected-surface map; walk every face that has a neighbor in a *different* selected surface (region-boundary faces — the original plan keyed on multi-hypothesis membership, but pruning/absorption destroy that; adjacency is the modern equivalent).
2. Compute the face's max vertex distance to its own surface and to each adjacent selected surface.
```
</details>

<!-- msg_011Cd6kTz78b5hV4KxA9L7jP -->

**Verified: the absorb pass is what's disturbing the geometry.** It's now stage **2.8**, boundary refinement is **2.9**, and both can be skipped.

Your questions, answered:

- **Did absorb have a pass number?** No — it ran unnumbered between 2.7 (NURBS) and boundary refinement, with its work silently folded into the "Stage 2.6" summary line. Boundary refinement was equally unnumbered.
- **Could it be skipped?** No — the parser rejected anything past 2.7. Now it can: absorb is **2.8**, boundary refinement is **2.9**, both honored by `--skip-stage` and by `--stage` (so `--stage=2.7` now stops before absorb — the test helpers that pinned `--stage=2.6`/`2.7` for post-absorb assertions were migrated to `--stage=2`, which is the same endpoint).

**The verification.** Absorb's loose rule merges any 1–2-face orphan into a bigger neighbor if its vertices lie within `--surface-tolerance` (0.4mm) — on benchy it merged ~128,000 of 169,890 facet surfaces, each then claiming to lie on a plane up to 0.4mm away. The A/B run with `--skip-stage=2.2,2.3,2.5,2.7,2.8`:

| | absorb on | absorb off |
|---|---|---|
| faces failing BRepCheck | 794 / 41,324 | **4** / 169,890 |
| solid BRepCheck | **failed** | **passes** |
| volume | 15,393.7 mm³ | **15,550.53 mm³** — matches the official 3DBenchy 15.55 cm³ exactly (absorb had eaten 1%) |
| STEP size / time | 704MB / ~5 min | 2.3GB / ~10 min |

Stage 2.9 is harmless in this mode (196 tolerance-level face moves between near-identical planes; the corner-refinement guard fired zero times), so **exact planar-only mode is `--skip-stage=2.2,2.3,2.5,2.7,2.8`**.

The 4 residual failures (0.002%) are different beasts, recorded in [TODO.md](TODO.md): face 40 is a two-wire planar face failing wire *imbrication* — the same multi-loop/disconnected-region family as the normal pipeline's face-5841 blocker — and three are micro-slivers (≤0.002mm²) with invalid wires. Neither is absorb-related.

All 494 tests pass and clippy is clean (the 2.6/2.7-pinning tests keep asserting exactly what they asserted before). Docs updated (README, DEVELOPMENT_PLAN, module header). Uncommitted — want me to commit?

> I need to handle two things: first, commit the current work following the project conventions—the build is clean with 494 passing tests, so I'll create a commit message with the model name and user prompts, then stage and commit the modified files. Second, I explain what the absorb pass does and how it helps the existing test models, though that explanation seems to be cut off in the thinking.
> 
> The absorb pass addresses three key issues: it catches spurious sub-fits where tight tolerance rules create narrow bands on curved surfaces that get committed before the real surface's breadth-first search reaches them (like azimuthal strips on a cone with nearly coplanar normals), it handles tangent-junction bridge facets that span boundaries between surfaces and can't be absorbed by either one, leaving orphaned single-face planar surfaces that break downstream stages, and it runs after the freeform gathering step so it doesn't hide freeform regions while still absorbing small accidental analytic fits like cylinder ribbons that naturally cluster with their surrounding facets.
> 
> To figure out which models actually benefit from this, I could run the test suite with the absorption stage skipped and see which ones fail — that would give a definitive empirical answer. matches the official 3DBenchy 15.55 cm^3 (absorb had eaten 1%).
```
Stage 2.9 is harmless in this mode (196 tolerance-level moves).

The 4 residual BRepCheck failures are unrelated to absorb and recorded
in TODO.md: one two-wire planar face failing wire imbrication (the
multi-loop/disconnected-region family, cf. face 5841) and three
micro-sliver faces (0.0004-0.0017 mm^2) with invalid wires.

Full suite (494 tests) green; clippy clean.
```

```
3b2b48c 2026-07-06 Full pipeline for fillet/cone models; deterministic fitting; absorption pass
===
Full pipeline for fillet/cone models; deterministic fitting; absorption pass

filleted_cylinder, filleted_pipe, filleted_hole_block, nosecone_medium,
cone_truncated_medium, and chamfered_cube ascii now pass --compare
through stage 4.1 (5 new stage-4.1 compare tests). All 443 tests green.

Stage 2:
- All fit_* functions take sorted vertex slices instead of HashSets.
  Float addition is non-associative, so HashSet iteration order made
  fits nondeterministic (simple_cone failed 1 run in 3).
- Torus minor-circle coverage is measured from face NORMALS (span ≥
  0.3 rad) instead of centroid positions. A flat annulus lies exactly
  on a one-parameter family of degenerate tori and can span a nonzero
  positional tube arc, but its normals are parallel; bogus tori were
  stealing filleted_pipe's annulus faces from the real planes.
- angular_tol_multiplier = 2.0 for tori: torus quads twist, so stage
  1.3 fusion fails on fillet patches, leaving triangles whose normals
  differ by ~2x the tessellation step (filleted_hole_block strips).
- New absorb_redundant_surfaces pass after selection merges (a)
  spurious sub-fits (cylinder bands on shallow cones, tol 10x vertex
  tolerance) and (b) tangent-junction bridge facets (<= 2 faces, tol =
  surface tolerance) into larger adjacent surfaces. Face centroids
  must also lie on the target: end caps triangulated as fans from a
  boundary vertex have ALL vertices on the adjacent cone's boundary
  circle, and a vertex-only test would absorb the whole cap.
- Selection fallback faces are logged at verbosity >= 2.

Stage 3:
- Sphere-cone tangent circles: NOT great circles (radius R·cos α,
  offset along the axis). Constructed perpendicular to the cone axis
  with position/radius averaged from mesh boundary vertices.
- Reoriented sphere faces compute V bounds by projecting boundary
  circle samples onto the oriented surface; fixed hemisphere bounds
  assumed a great-circle junction and misplaced cone boundaries.
- Torus face V-bounds: mesh centroids only disambiguate which arc the
  face spans; including them in the window computation shifted the
  boundary ~2 degrees past the tangent circle (junction-ring cap
  triangles), breaking sewing nondeterministically.
- validate_faces wraps detailed BRepCheck diagnostics in catch_unwind:
  BRepCheck_Face/Edge can throw Standard_NullObject on invalid faces,
  which previously aborted the pipeline.

Tests:
- rounded_cube_coarse stage3/4 tests pass --angular-tolerance=20 (the
  coarse tessellation exceeds the 17.5 default; RESULTS.md documents
  this flag).
- simple_cone hypothesis-count test moved to stage 2.6 selection.

Docs: DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md, LEARNINGS.md, TODO.md, DONE.md updated to
match the implementation; remaining failures (pipe_elbow,
```

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011Cd74ZmdZNheUnBDWV6bYv -->

Committed as `eb7dd65`. Here's what absorb is for, with its design intent verified against what it actually does across the current test corpus (measured today by A/B-running ~12 models with `--compare`, absorb on vs `--skip-stage=2.8`).

## What absorb is for

It was added in commit `3b2b48c` (2026-07-06, the fillet/cone-models session) to clean up two specific failure modes of greedy surface selection — both of which leave a selected surface that merely *duplicates a neighbor's geometry*, creating fake edges and corner vertices that break stage 3:

1. **Spurious sub-fits** (the tight rule, 10× vertex tolerance ≈ 5e-4mm): a narrow band of faces on a cone or sphere can fit an "exact" cylinder — e.g. three azimuthal strips of a shallow cone have nearly coplanar normals — and gets committed before the real surface's BFS ever reaches those faces. Since every vertex of the band lies exactly on the real surface, merging it in is lossless.

2. **Tangent-junction bridge facets** (the loose rule, `--surface-tolerance` = 0.4mm, restricted to ≤2-face orphans merging into >2-face targets): a facet straddling a G1 junction (the nosecone's cone–sphere tangency) has vertices on both sides, so neither surface's BFS can claim it at vertex tolerance. It survives as an orphan single-face planar surface punching a fake face into the junction circle. Its chordal sag off either real surface is bounded by tessellation tolerance — hence the loose threshold, which is exactly the slop that wrecked planar-only benchy.

Two guards shape it: centroids must also lie on the target (else fan-triangulated end caps get eaten — all their *vertices* lie on the adjacent cylinder's boundary circle), and the loose rule requires a >2-face target so orphan-to-orphan chaining can't dissolve the freeform regions stage 2.7 needs (that's also why 2.7 runs before 2.8).

## What it actually helps with today

| Models | Absorb's role |
|---|---|
| **nosecone_truncated** | **Load-bearing.** Absorbs 3 spurious sub-fits; without it, 3 bogus cones survive and a fake vertex lands 80.6mm off the model — stage 3.1 compare hard-fails. |
| nosecone_medium | Absorbs 1 bridge facet, but no longer *needed* — boundary refinement (2.9) backstops it, same final 3 surfaces either way. |
| stepped_block_toroidal | Absorbs 2 bridge facets; without it they're just 2 extra tiny planar faces and everything still passes. Cosmetic. |
| filleted_cylinder / filleted_pipe / filleted_hole_block, pill_fine, chamfered_cube, block_with_hole, saddle | **Idle — 0 merges.** Notably this includes the very fillet models that motivated the pass; the later junction-mask, normal-coverage, and seed-ordering work now prevents those spurious fits upstream. |

So absorb has narrowed from "load-bearing for the fillet family" to a rescue mechanism for essentially one model, plus cosmetic cleanup at tangent junctions — while being actively harmful when the mesh itself is the ground truth (planar-only benchy). If you ever want to retire or tighten it (e.g. drop the loose rule, keep the tight one), nosecone_truncated is the test that must keep passing.

Two drive-by findings, flagged as a background-task chip rather than fixed now: **nosecone_truncated has no test coverage at all** (and fails the 4.1 volume gate at 1.38% even with absorb — pre-existing), and **hill_with_hole fails the 3.6 volume gate at 1.11% with default flags** (absorb-independent; it's only suite-tested at stage 1, and a 2026-07-15 note recorded it at ~0.4%, so it may be a recent regression worth a bisect).

```bash
python3 - <<'EOF'
import struct, math

# Fitted geometry (from brepper -vv, errors ~1e-6 on this CAD tessellation)
C = (0.0, -59.5, 0.0)                      # sphere center, r=12.5
A = (0.0, -41.7617, 28.6899)               # cone apex
r = 12.5
u = tuple(A[i]-C[i] for i in range(3))     # axis: center -> apex
d = math.sqrt(sum(x*x for x in u))
u = tuple(x/d for x in u)
s_j = r*r/d                                # tangency plane axial coord (from C toward apex)

def s(p):  # axial coordinate of point p
    return sum((p[i]-C[i])*u[i] for i in range(3))

with open('tests/onshape/nosecone_medium.stl','rb') as f:
    f.read(80)
    n = struct.unpack('<I', f.read(4))[0]
    tris = []
    for _ in range(n):
        data = struct.unpack('<12fH', f.read(50))
        v = [data[3:6], data[6:9], data[9:12]]
        tris.append(v)

EPS = 1e-3
straddle, on_ring_verts, touch = 0, set(), [0,0]
ring_tris_sphere, ring_tris_cone = 0, 0
min_gap_pos, min_gap_neg = 1e9, -1e9
examples = []
for t in tris:
    ss = [s(v) - s_j for v in t]
    pos = sum(1 for x in ss if x >  EPS)
    neg = sum(1 for x in ss if x < -EPS)
    ring = sum(1 for x in ss if abs(x) <= EPS)
    for v,x in zip(t,ss):
        if abs(x) <= EPS: on_ring_verts.add(tuple(round(c,6) for c in v))
        elif x > 0: min_gap_pos = min(min_gap_pos, x)
        else:       min_gap_neg = max(min_gap_neg, x)
    if pos and neg:
        straddle += 1
        if len(examples) < 3: examples.append([round(x,4) for x in ss])
    if ring and pos: ring_tris_sphere += 1
    if ring and neg: ring_tris_cone += 1

print(f"triangles: {len(tris)}")
print(f"tangency plane s_j = {s_j:.4f} mm, ring radius = {r*math.sqrt(1-(r/d)**2):.4f} mm")
print(f"STRADDLING triangles (verts strictly both sides, eps={EPS}): {straddle}")
if examples: print("  example vertex offsets:", examples)
print(f"distinct vertices ON the ring (|s-s_j|<=eps): {len(on_ring_verts)}")
print(f"triangles touching ring from sphere side: {ring_tris_sphere}, cone side: {ring_tris_cone}")
print(f"closest strictly-off-ring vertex: +{min_gap_pos:.4f} / {min_gap_neg:.4f} mm")
EOF
```

```bash
    (1369, (-46.4547,-113.7500,-31.3489), (1.000,0.000,0.000)),

    (1590, (-35.6725,-113.7500,-42.0934), (0.000,0.000,1.000)),
    (1591, (-69.7537,-113.7500,-34.2009), (-1.000,0.000,0.000)),
    (1592, (-66.1075,-113.7500,-15.2009), (0.000,0.000,1.000)),
    (1801, (-26.8805,-113.7500,-49.4891), (1.000,0.000,0.000)),
]

with open('tests/onshape/stepped_block_toroidal_fillets_medium.stl','rb') as f:
    f.read(80)
    n = struct.unpack('<I', f.read(4))[0]
    tris = []
    for _ in range(n):
        d = struct.unpack('<12fH', f.read(50))
        tris.append((d[0:3], [d[3:6], d[6:9], d[9:12]]))

def norm(v):
    l = math.sqrt(sum(x*x for x in v)); return tuple(x/l for x in v) if l else v

for fid, c, nrm in orphans:
    nrm = norm(nrm)
    verts = set()
    area = 0.0
    for tn, tv in tris:
        tn = norm(tn)
        if abs(sum(a*b for a,b in zip(tn,nrm))) < 0.99997: continue  # ~0.4 deg
        # same plane through the centroid?
        if abs(sum((tv[0][k]-c[k])*nrm[k] for k in range(3))) > 2e-3: continue
        # near the centroid (same face, not a distant coplanar face)
        cx = [sum(v[k] for v in tv)/3 for k in range(3)]
        if max(abs(cx[k]-c[k]) for k in range(3)) > 60: continue
        for v in tv: verts.add(tuple(round(x,5) for x in v))
        u = [tv[1][k]-tv[0][k] for k in range(3)]; w = [tv[2][k]-tv[0][k] for k in range(3)]
        cr = (u[1]*w[2]-u[2]*w[1], u[2]*w[0]-u[0]*w[2], u[0]*w[1]-u[1]*w[0])
        area += 0.5*math.sqrt(sum(x*x for x in cr))
    if not verts: print(f"face {fid}: no match"); continue
    vs = list(verts)
    # extents along two in-plane axes
    ref = (0,1,0)
    t1 = norm(tuple(nrm[1]*ref[2]-nrm[2]*ref[1] for _ in [0]) if False else norm((nrm[1]*ref[2]-nrm[2]*ref[1], nrm[2]*ref[0]-nrm[0]*ref[2], nrm[0]*ref[1]-nrm[1]*ref[0])))
    t2 = norm((nrm[1]*t1[2]-nrm[2]*t1[1], nrm[2]*t1[0]-nrm[0]*t1[2], nrm[0]*t1[1]-nrm[1]*t1[0]))
    e1 = [sum((v[k]-c[k])*t1[k] for k in range(3)) for v in vs]
    e2 = [sum((v[k]-c[k])*t2[k] for k in range(3)) for v in vs]
    print(f"face {fid}: n={nrm[0]:+.3f},{nrm[1]:+.3f},{nrm[2]:+.3f} area={area:7.3f} mm^2  "
          f"nverts={len(vs)}  extent {max(e1)-min(e1):6.2f} x {max(e2)-min(e2):6.2f} mm")
EOF
```

<!-- msg_011Cd7hwUwsx89dugLyweSuw -->

**You're right, and I was confused** — and so is the code's own documentation, which is where my claim came from.

## What the evidence shows

**Your hypothesis holds on this model.** I computed the exact tangency circle from the fitted geometry (the cone is precisely the tangent cone to the r=12.5 sphere: sin α = r/d to 4 digits; ring at 4.632mm axial offset, radius 11.61mm) and classified every raw STL triangle against it:

- **Zero triangles straddle the junction.**
- **104 vertices lie exactly on the tangency ring** (nearest interior vertex is 0.048mm away), with 182 triangles touching the ring from the sphere side and 162 from the cone side.

Onshape emitted a clean seam along the tangent edge, exactly as you predicted. Per-face tessellation with shared boundary vertices, tangency notwithstanding.

## What absorb actually does on the nosecones

Not bridge facets. Reading the `-vv` absorb lines: every merge on both nosecones is the **tight rule** (10× vertex tolerance), with source vertices lying on the target to ~1e-5mm:

- **nosecone_medium**: one 6-face *bogus cylinder* (r=12.4992) fitted to a quad band of the r=12.5 sphere — the classic spurious sub-fit.
- **nosecone_truncated**: three merges — a 6-face bogus cone, a 7-face bogus cylinder (again a sphere band), and, most interestingly, a **31-face fragment of the real cone** (identical apex and half-angle to the main 117-face cone hypothesis) getting reunited with its main body after seeding split it.

So absorb's real, verified value in the corpus is entirely the tight rule: exact-overlap dedup of accidental sub-fits and split-hypothesis fragments.

## Where the "bridge facet" story came from, and what the loose rule really eats

The `absorb_redundant_surfaces` doc comment and DEVELOPMENT_PLAN step 6 describe "a mesh facet spanning a tangent junction (e.g. cone–sphere on the nosecone)" — I repeated that. It's false for the current corpus. The loose rule's only actual firings today are on stepped_block, and I probed those too: the two absorbed surfaces (84.63 and 127.29mm², matching the STL exactly) are **real B-rep planar faces** — narrow wall strips 0.86mm and 1.29mm wide × 98.5mm long that Onshape tessellated as a single rectangle each. One rectangle → one fused quad → orphan, because BFS planar hypotheses require ≥2 faces. The loose rule then *replaces these real faces* with neighboring geometry 0.12–0.27mm away — quiet harm inside the 0.4mm gate, the same failure mode that wrecked planar-only benchy.

## Implications (not yet acted on)

1. The absorb doc comment and plan text carry a stale/false motivation and should be corrected.
2. The loose rule now has no demonstrated benefit anywhere in the corpus and two demonstrated harms — a deletion or drastic-tightening candidate.
3. The root gap it papered over: a real face tessellated as one rectangle can't become a planar hypothesis (≥2-face rule) — yet before stage 1.3 fusion it was *two coplanar triangles*, which would have qualified. Letting a single fused quad stand as a legitimate planar hypothesis would fix the actual problem.

Whether a true straddling facet ever existed back in July when the pass was written would take archaeology in that commit's agent logs — but for the current pipeline and models, your model of tessellator behavior is correct. Want me to fix the docs and/or try removing the loose rule against the suite?

Tolerance: `--surface-tolerance` (chordal sag), only for surfaces with ≤ 2 faces.

```markdown
6. **Absorb redundant surfaces** (`absorb_redundant_surfaces`; CLI sub-stage **2.8**, skippable via `--skip-stage=2.8`, e.g. for exact planar-only conversion where the chordal slop of the loose rule would put absorbed facets up to `--surface-tolerance` off their assigned surface): merge a selected surface S into an adjacent, larger-area surface T when all of S's vertices lie within tolerance of T's fitted geometry AND all of S's face centroids lie within `--surface-tolerance` of T.    - *Loose rule* (`--surface-tolerance`, only for S with ≤ 2 faces into T with > 2 faces). **Its original motivation ("tangent-junction bridge facets" — a facet straddling a tangent junction with vertices on both surfaces) was tested 2026-07-17 and is false for the current corpus**: CAD tessellators emit seam edges along B-rep face boundaries even where surfaces are tangent (nosecone cone–sphere tangency: 0 straddling STL triangles; 104 vertices exactly on the tangency ring).
```
